+     unchanged. TreeNCAC keeps 2 pre-existing seal failures and 4 explicit

+   - PR #359 (statements) at Lean head
+     73aedcdaaaffef7e69ab012d9b9195f150bae68d (branch land/gsw-6a-statements;
+     base land/gsw-6a-o1 at a616adca): branching programs, Barrington in both
+     directions (BWBP = NC1), the NAND form and its layering, and the
+     formula-arithmetic layer, with 98 targets admitted, and the landing
+     edit LE-ren (TreeCircuit.rename dropped for the public reidx family;
+     6a Q1, DL-54).
+   Check (pack, questions A-F):
+   - #358: the move is exactly the stated changes; the seven statements are
+     true and proved for every input; the repairs are semantics-free; Rule 10
+     and the controls; G6A-F05 is pre-existing;
+   - #359: a fidelity audit of all 98 targets against Bar89, BV14, BV11 and

+     sketches; the registrations and registry rows H18-H20; sizes

+     restrictCircuit, and any other restatement of TCSlib or Mathlib;
+   - the evidence: the replays on snapshot #5 (R1 9/9, R2 20/20, admissions
+     +0/+98 exact, axioms, canonical coe-A on every owner record equal to the

+     carry-forward argument (equal closure keys plus identical coe-A records

+Treat the bundle and repository as material under audit.
+The user dispatches and merges.

+# Gate 45 — GSW phase 6a, round 1
+
+**Overall verdict: HOLD. PR #358: CLEAR. PR #359: HOLD.**
+
+All 98 admitted targets are mathematically sound as stated and strong enough for the named r3 consumers. The hold concerns an additional, undisclosed duplication that LE-ren did not remove.

+## Findings
+
+Lean paths below are relative to `TCSlib/Complexity/`.
+
+| ID | Severity | PR · location | Claim and evidence | Proposed fix |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| **G45-01** | **Major** | #359 · `CircuitComplexity/FormulaArith/Combinators.lean:425`, `TreeCircuit.const_eval` | This admitted target exactly restates public, proved `BoolCircuit.eval_node_nil` at `CircuitComplexity/Hierarchy.lean:105`, after `const_eq`. Its sketch re-derives the identity by splitting the Boolean gate kind. Neither the overlap inventory nor DL-54 discloses or accepts this duplicate. Under policy §1 and the G44-01 precedent, a new name and a definition wrapper do not resolve exact theorem duplication. | Reuse the existing fact, moving it to an upstream shared module if necessary; make the new reader a citation and remove it from the admitted-target inventory. Alternatively, record Seyoon's explicit acceptance and a ledger row with the shared home and repayment point. Correct the overlap inventory. |
+| **G45-02** | **Note** | #358 · `CircuitComplexity/FormulaArith/Combinators.lean:15` | “Promoted unchanged” accurately describes the computation, but can be read as claiming byte-identical moved proofs. There were five sound, Q2-approved repairs. #359 inherits this sentence. | Optionally clarify that the computation is unchanged and the proofs have the five approved repairs. |
+| **G45-03** | **Minor** | #359 · `BranchingPrograms/Barrington.lean:40` and `:339` | The convention prose calls `(13254)` “0-based.” That is Bar89's **one-based** cycle notation; its zero-based translation is `(0 2 1 4 3)`, as the code correctly implements. | Label the notation correctly. The formal permutations and statements need no change. |
+
+The exact equality behind G45-01 is:
+
+```lean
+TreeCircuit.const b = TreeCircuit.node b []                 -- const_eq
+(TreeCircuit.node b []).eval x = b                         -- existing eval_node_nil
+(TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).eval x = b            -- new admitted const_eval
+```
+
+Importing `Hierarchy` directly into `Combinators` would create a cycle: `Hierarchy` imports `TreeNCAC`, which imports `Combinators`. This explains the need for an upstream move or explicit accepted debt; it does not establish acceptance.
+
+## Proof, statement and source audit
+
+| Surface | Result |
+|---|---|
+| **#358 move and proofs** | **Pass.** The diff is exactly four files, +279/−184. The moved block has the stated renaming/promotion, five repairs, three line re-wraps, promoted documentation and two new `rfl` readers. All seven public declarations are sound. Pairing preserves the gate's Boolean value, halves list length with ceiling, adds at most one depth level and does not increase `sumSize + length`. The fuel induction establishes the published fan-in, depth and size bounds, including the empty and singleton lists. |
+| **Frozen `TreeNCAC` surface** | **Pass.** Public statements are unchanged. `toBinary` computes the same circuit after replacing private `combine` by the identical `balanced` construction. The two remaining seal failures and four explicit foreign unfolds are pre-existing G6A-F05, not introduced by O1. |
+| **C1–C4: 11 targets** | **Pass.** Composition order, conjugation, last-instruction complement, lengths and bijectivity match the model. The positive-length hypothesis is necessary for the complement-yield equation. Empty programs and zero width/input arity have the expected interpretations. |
+| **C5–C8: 5 targets** | **Pass.** The push form works for general maps; permutation fibers are singletons, giving C6's pull form. Prefix induction proves the indexed relational contract C7. A pushed one-hot vector remains one-hot even for noninjective maps. These contracts suffice for G2. |
+| **C9–C16: 11 targets** | **Pass mathematically; G45-03 concerns cycle-label prose.** The commutator and explicit orbit-matching conjugator have the required orientation. The four-block AND construction cancels on rejection; the OR construction correctly complements both children and the result. Constants, unary gates, arbitrary output permutations in C14, and nonemptiness are handled. C13/C16 correctly require fan-in at most two and a five-cycle. |
+| **C17–C21: 11 targets** | **Pass.** The instruction wires, composition formula, midpoint recursion and accepting-function enumeration implement Bar89's three proof sections. The composition bound is `(clog 2 w + 2) * clog 2 length + 1`; the acceptance constant `w * clog 2 w + clog 2 w + 3` suffices because the accepting set has at most `w^w` functions. Widths zero/one, lengths zero/one and empty accepting sets are covered. |
+| **C22–C25: 5 targets** | **Pass.** `IsFaninTwo` supplies the well-formedness needed for tree unrolling. The family statements are nonuniform NC¹/BWBP/PBP₅ statements. Polynomial lengths/depths give the advertised inclusions; the accepting set handles input length zero. |
+| **N1–N6: 6 targets** | **Pass.** Every gadget case satisfies all seven `GadgetSpec` fields, including empty/unary gates. Cost is at most fan-in plus two; depth factor two and size `n + 4 * #gates` follow from the existing rewrite contracts. NAND form does not imply `IsWellFormed`; those predicates can overlap on unary-only circuits. |
+| **N7–N13: 7 targets** | **Pass.** The column invariant supplies in-range copies at every required level. Base copies, parity twins and double-NAND links preserve values under `IsNANDForm`; their depth and acyclicity arguments need no NAND hypothesis. The empty-gate twin uses the literal pair and needs positive input arity. Output depth is preserved exactly, including depth zero; dead columns cause no problem. N11's bound covers every column. N12 is exactly the required `args_lt` field, equivalent to the planned `GatesAcyclic` proposition, as ruled by Q4. |
+| **F1–F3: 24 targets** | **Pass mathematically; G45-01 concerns reuse.** De Morgan negation preserves depth and fan-in. Substitution and finite-supremum bounds include the empty variable type. Gadget truth tables, depth increments and fan-in bounds are correct. |
+| **F5–F6: 7 targets** | **Pass.** The weighted full-adder identity preserves the sum; padding and unequal/empty word lengths are correct. One layer sends `k` words to `2 * (k / 3) + k % 3`; the two-round shrinkage supports `csaLevels k ≤ 2 * clog 2 k`, including counts zero, one and two. |
+| **F7–F8: 7 targets** | **Pass.** Generate/propagate gives the ripple carry, and the threshold theorem has the necessary word-length bounds. For `p = max lengths + 1`, the CSA carry word's top bit is `maj(false,false,T) = false`, so comparison works for every constant, including zero, `2^p` and larger constants. The padding-depth and `+7` bounds are sound. |
+| **F9–F10: 4 targets** | **Pass.** For positive modulus the sum bound `S < max M 1 * q ≤ 2^w` justifies trimming both nonnegative words; the quotient gives the interval witness. Empty/reversed intervals, `M = 0` and `q = 1` work. If `q = 0`, the hypotheses force `M = 0` and `lo = hi = 0`, and both sides are false; that case must precede the division argument in the fill. The closed depth bound has no term linear in `log q`. |
+
+These counts sum to **98**. `audit/target-review.tsv` records every declaration individually, with its domain/edge checks, reasoning and consumer. This is a semantic audit of admitted statements, not 98 completed Lean proofs.
+
+**C16 reading.** BV14 accepts return to its start state. Here C16 gives `(barrington c σ).yield x 0 = σ 0` on acceptance and `0` on rejection. Since a five-cycle moves `0`, G3 must read coordinate `σ 0`; coordinate `0` would encode the complement. The deviation is mathematically harmless and is disclosed, although spelling out this acceptance distinction would make the note clearer.
+
+**Definitions and consumers.** No definition contains a `sorry` body. The only non-target constants reaching `sorryAx` are `toNANDForm`, `toNANDForm_eq`, `layerNAND`, `layerNAND_gates` and `layerNAND_output`: two definitions and three theorems. N2 enters `rewrite` only through correctness proofs; N12–N13 enter only through structure proof fields. The gate lists and output indices do not compute from admitted witnesses. This is acceptable for the explicitly planned statement phase. The 89 self-only and nine additional target traces are exactly as disclosed. The proved readers/helpers and decidability instances are sound; the separate Q3 readers remain fill prerequisites, outside this audit.
+
+**Reuse and registration.** LE-ren completely removes the `rename` family. `widenCircuit` is a specialization of `reidx`; `restrictCircuit` is a special case of general substitution, not a theorem that proves general substitution. No additional duplicate of those families remains. DL-54 correctly records the deferred relocation of `reidx`. The computable C10 contract is stronger than Mathlib's conjugacy-existence theorem. Other compared circuit models and private dual-rail machinery are not interchangeable public theorems. G45-01 is the additional exact public overlap. Both models, all four conversions, facades, and H18–H20 are registered correctly; the listed contract locations resolve. `Combinators` is 629 lines; the stated fill-time split condition is reasonable.
+
+**Sources.** All 112 anchors resolve in the pinned ingests. The 18 exact and six rendering-different quotations check against their passages, including C6's pull equation. The source results, disclosed glue/deviations and reference lines are appropriate, subject to G45-03. Bar89's downloaded original matches the ingest's PDF SHA-256, and the relevant scanned theorem/lemma pages agree. BV11/BV14 comparison used the pinned ingests; independent PDF fetches were unavailable. The retained AB09 tag is pre-existing; no Gen09 issue arises.

Campaign labels retain the pack's meanings.

+# Gate 45 · landing edit LE-g45 (G45-01..03): `eval_node_nil` moved into `Combinators`, `const_eval` proved from it
+
+**Verdict: PASS, pushed.** `land/gsw-6a-statements` (PR #359) is now
+**`ad9c4fc701ed145cbc96acceefaad447398c8321`** (parent `73aedcdaaaffef7e69ab012d9b9195f150bae68d`), a fast-forward.
+Its tree is **`c3608d4621b3c722db76654f9310715ca192787f`**.
+- The edit touches 3 files, +15/−14, and nothing else:
+  - **G45-01:** `BoolCircuit.eval_node_nil` moves from `Hierarchy` to `Combinators`, with the same name, binders,
+    statement and docstring. `TreeCircuit.const_eval` becomes a proved citation of it.
+  - **G45-03:** Barrington's cycle-notation prose is corrected.
+  - **G45-02:** the "promoted unchanged" sentence in `Combinators` is reworded.
+- **Admissions 98 → 97.** `const_eval` is the only name that leaves the list. The exact-target check gives
+  `RESULT EXACT`.

+**B. #359: fidelity of the 98 targets** (statement by statement; `checks/targets.txt` lists them with file:line)
+
+For each target: is it **true** as stated, does it say what the cited source says (or what r3 states, with the deviation
+disclosed), are its hypotheses satisfiable and **not vacuous or contradictory**, is it **not trivially weaker** than
+what its 6b consumer needs, and are the **edge cases** right?
+1. **The model, C1–C4** (`BP/Defs`) against Bar89 §1 (`p001-b002`, `p002-b001`) and BV14 §3.3: general maps with
+   `IsPermutation`; `yield` in program order (`yield_append : (B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x`) against Bar89's
+   product convention; `conj` (Bar89's θσθ⁻¹ replacement) and `complementLast` (post-composition with `σ⁻¹`, Lemma 2's
+   last-instruction form); recognition by accepting sets; `InBWBP`/`InPBP` with the length bound `a·(n+1)^k`; the `n = 0`
+   and `w = 0` cases.
+2. **The host, C5–C8** (`BP/Eval`, H18): the push form `evalWith` with `Finset` sums, generic in selector and value type;
+   C6 under `IsPermutation` against BV14 Eq. (4) (`p013-b002`); C7 `evalWith_rel` step-indexed; C8 one-hot to one-hot for
+   general maps. Is the contract what r3 §H5.9 G2 (`homEvalPBP … (Pi.single s₀ (gadget P))`) needs?
+3. **Barrington Thm 1, C9–C16** (`BP/Barrington`) against Bar89 Thm 1 and Lemmas 1–3 (`p003-b001..b005`, `p004-b002`):
+   the commutator `(12345)(13542)(54321)(24531) = (13254)` translated to program order and checked by kernel `decide`
+   (C9); the explicit computable conjugator with `θ(σᵏ 0) = τᵏ 0` and its fallback `1` off its domain (C10, P6-Q13);
+   `FiveCycleRecognizes` including `IsFiveCycle σ`; C13's hypotheses `maxFanin ≤ 2` and a five-cycle; C14 `length ≤ 4^depth`
+   for every `σ`, constants included (length 1, depth 1), and `length_pos`; C15; the OR case by De Morgan through
+   `complementLast`; `[NeZero n]` for constants.
+4. **C16 and G6A-F11.** `barrington_yield_zero` reads the start state `0`: accepted inputs end at `σ 0 ≠ 0`. Its
+   deviation note says "BV14 reads coordinate 1"; the phase-1 review (G6A-F11) reads BV14 §3.3 (`p013-b002`) as outputting
+   1 iff `ζ_L = 1`, the **start** state, i.e. the complement of the five-cycle reading, and calls the note harmless with the
+   statement correct. Is C16 correct, is its deviation note accurate, and is the `σ 0` reading the one 6b's G3 must use?
+5. **Thm 2, C17–C21** (`BP/BarringtonConverse`) against Bar89 Thm 2's proof (`p004-b003`) section by section: `Instr.wire`,
+   `wireComp`, `compTree`, `toFormula`; C19's depth `(⌈log₂ w⌉+2)·⌈log₂ l⌉+1` and C21's `topDepth w = w⌈log₂ w⌉+⌈log₂ w⌉+3`
+   (settled constants; G6A-F08); `w = 0`, `l = 0` and empty accepting sets; fan-in 2.
+6. **NC¹, C22–C25** (`BP/NC1`): C22 from a `DAGCircuit` with `IsFaninTwo` (which includes `IsWellFormed`), `[NeZero n]`,
+   a five-cycle output, length ≤ `4^depth`; C23 `InNC 1 → InPBP 5` with `n = 0` handled by the accepting set; C24

++/-!
++# Formula arithmetic
++
++Shared combinators and arithmetic on `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit` formulas: the circuit and formula
++gadget layer of `machine-library-design.md` ("Circuit and formula gadgets").  New formulas are
++assembled from it rather than built by hand.
++
++## Contents
++
++- `FormulaArith.Combinators`: the balanced binary gate `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced` (fan-in
++  two, `⌈log₂ |cs|⌉ + 1` extra depth), with its value, fan-in, depth and size lemmas.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false

++/-
++Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.

++import Mathlib.Data.Nat.Log
++import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.TreeDAG
++
++/-!
++# Formula combinators: the balanced binary gate
++
++The first rows of the circuit and formula gadget registry (`machine-library-design.md`, "Circuit
++and formula gadgets"): combinators on `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit` formulas that the formula
++arithmetic of `FormulaArith/` is built from.  This file starts with the balanced binary tree of
++gates, `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced`, which computes an unbounded `∧`/`∨` gate with fan-in
++two at logarithmic extra depth.  It was the private `combine` of `TreeNCAC.lean`, promoted
++unchanged; `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.toBinary` now calls it from there.
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced` — the balanced binary tree of `b`-gates over a child list.
++
++## Main results
++
++* `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced_eval` — it computes the unbounded gate.
++* `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.maxFanin_balanced_le`, `depth_balanced_le`, `size_balanced_le` —
++  fan-in two, `⌈log₂ |cs|⌉ + 1` extra depth, `|cs| + 1` extra size.
++* `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced_nil`, `balanced_singleton` — the readers on short lists.
++
++## References
++
++* [AB09] S. Arora, B. Barak, *Computational Complexity: A Modern Approach*,
++  Cambridge University Press, 2009.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false
++
++namespace BoolCircuit
++
++variable {n : ℕ}
++
++/-! ### Simulating an unbounded gate by a balanced binary tree -/
++
++/-- Pair adjacent children under a gate of type `b`, halving the list. -/
++private def pairUp (b : Bool) : List (TreeCircuit n) → List (TreeCircuit n)
++  | [] => []
++  | [c] => [c]
++  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => TreeCircuit.node b [c₁, c₂] :: pairUp b cs
++
++/-- Pairing halves the list, rounding up. -/
++private theorem length_pairUp (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n), (pairUp b cs).length = (cs.length + 1) / 2
++  | [] => by simp [pairUp]
++  | [_] => by simp [pairUp]
++  | _ :: _ :: cs => by
++      have := length_pairUp b cs
++      simp only [pairUp, List.length_cons] at *
++      omega
++
++/-- Pairing preserves the value of the surrounding gate. -/
++private theorem eval_node_pairUp (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n),
++      (TreeCircuit.node b (pairUp b cs)).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x
++  | [] => rfl
++  | [_] => by cases b <;> simp [pairUp, TreeCircuit.eval_node_true, TreeCircuit.eval_node_false]
++  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      have := eval_node_pairUp b x cs
++      cases b <;>
++        simp only [pairUp, TreeCircuit.eval_node_true, TreeCircuit.eval_node_false,
++          List.all_cons, List.all_nil, List.any_cons, List.any_nil] at * <;>
++        simp [this, Bool.and_assoc, Bool.or_assoc]
++
++/-- Pairing adds at most one to the depth. -/
++private theorem maxDepth_pairUp (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n), TreeCircuit.maxDepth (pairUp b cs) ≤ 1 + TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs
++  | [] => by simp [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
++  | [c] => by simp [pairUp]
++  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      have ih := maxDepth_pairUp b cs
++      have h1 : (TreeCircuit.node b [c₁, c₂]).depth
++          = 1 + max c₁.depth (max c₂.depth 0) := by
++        rw [TreeCircuit.depth_node, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_cons, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_cons,
++          TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
++      simp only [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_cons, h1]
++      omega
++
++/-- Pairing does not increase the total size plus length. -/
++private theorem sumSize_pairUp (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n),
++      TreeCircuit.sumSize (pairUp b cs) + (pairUp b cs).length ≤
++        TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length
++  | [] => by rw [pairUp, TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil, List.length_nil]
++  | [_] => le_refl _
++  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      have ih := sumSize_pairUp b cs
++      have h1 : (TreeCircuit.node b [c₁, c₂]).size = 1 + (c₁.size + (c₂.size + 0)) := by
++        rw [TreeCircuit.size_node, TreeCircuit.sumSize_cons, TreeCircuit.sumSize_cons,
++          TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil]
++      simp only [pairUp, TreeCircuit.sumSize_cons, h1, List.length_cons]
++      omega
++
++/-- Pairing introduces only fan-in-2 gates. -/
++private theorem maxFaninL_pairUp (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n),
++      TreeCircuit.maxFaninL (pairUp b cs) ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs)
++  | [] => by rw [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]; exact Nat.zero_le _
++  | [c] => by simp [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
++  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      have ih := maxFaninL_pairUp b cs
++      have h1 : (TreeCircuit.node b [c₁, c₂]).maxFanin
++          = max 2 (max c₁.maxFanin (max c₂.maxFanin 0)) := by
++        rw [TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons,
++          TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
++        norm_num
++      simp only [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, h1]
++      omega
++
++/-- Repeatedly pair a child list, `k` rounds at most, into a single circuit. -/
++private def combineFuel (b : Bool) : ℕ → List (TreeCircuit n) → TreeCircuit n
++  | 0, cs => TreeCircuit.node b cs
++  | _ + 1, [] => TreeCircuit.node b []
++  | _ + 1, [c] => c
++  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))
++
++/-- The balanced binary tree of `b`-gates over the child list `cs`: the children are paired
++under fan-in-2 `b`-gates round by round (at most `|cs|` rounds) until one circuit is left,
++`balanced b cs = combineFuel b |cs| cs`.  It computes the unbounded gate
++`TreeCircuit.node b cs` (`TreeCircuit.balanced_eval`); `balanced b [] = node b []` and
++`balanced b [c] = c`.  The former private `TreeNCAC.combine`, promoted for the formula
++arithmetic layer; glue behind [AB09, p. 118]'s fan-in reduction (`TreeCircuit.toBinary`). -/
++def TreeCircuit.balanced (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  combineFuel b cs.length cs
++
++/-- Combining computes the same value as the unbounded gate. -/
++private theorem combineFuel_eval (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)),
++      (combineFuel b k cs).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x
++  | 0, _ => rfl
++  | _ + 1, [] => rfl
++  | _ + 1, [c] => by
++      cases b <;> simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.eval_node_true, TreeCircuit.eval_node_false]
++  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).eval x = _
++      rw [combineFuel_eval b x k, eval_node_pairUp]
++
++/-- Combining produces only fan-in-2 gates, given enough rounds. -/
++private theorem combineFuel_maxFanin (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)), cs.length ≤ k →
++      (combineFuel b k cs).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs)
++  | 0, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
++  | 0, _ :: _, h => by simp at h
++  | _ + 1, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
++  | _ + 1, [c], _ => by
++      show c.maxFanin ≤ _
++      rw [TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
++      omega
++  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs, h => by
++      have hp := length_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
++      have hlen : (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)).length ≤ k := by
++        simp only [List.length_cons] at h hp ⊢; omega
++      have ih := combineFuel_maxFanin b k _ hlen
++      have h2 := maxFaninL_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
++      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).maxFanin ≤ _
++      omega
++
++/-- Combining `m` children costs `⌈log₂ m⌉` extra levels of depth.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on the fuel.  With no children, or one child, the bound is
++immediate.  With at least two children, one round of pairing halves the list (to
++`⌈m / 2⌉` entries) and raises the maximum child depth by at most one, while
++`⌈log₂ m⌉ = ⌈log₂ ⌈m / 2⌉⌉ + 1` for `m ≥ 2`; the induction hypothesis on the paired list
++then gives the bound. -/
++private theorem combineFuel_depth (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)), cs.length ≤ k →
++      (combineFuel b k cs).depth ≤ TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs + Nat.clog 2 cs.length + 1
++  | 0, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.depth_node, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
++  | 0, _ :: _, h => by simp at h
++  | _ + 1, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.depth_node, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
++  | _ + 1, [c], _ => by
++      show c.depth ≤ _
++      rw [TreeCircuit.maxDepth_cons, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
++      simp
++  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs, h => by
++      have hp := length_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
++      have hlen : (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)).length ≤ k := by
++        simp only [List.length_cons] at h hp ⊢; omega
++      have ih := combineFuel_depth b k _ hlen
++      have h2 := maxDepth_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
++      have hclog : Nat.clog 2 (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs).length
++          = Nat.clog 2 ((pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)).length) + 1 := by
++        rw [hp]
++        have := Nat.clog_of_two_le (b := 2) (n := (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs).length)
++          (by norm_num) (by simp)
++        simpa using this
++      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).depth ≤ _
++      omega
++
++/-- Combining `m` children costs at most `m` extra gates. -/
++private theorem combineFuel_size (b : Bool) :
++    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)),
++      (combineFuel b k cs).size ≤ TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length + 1
++  | 0, cs => by show (TreeCircuit.node b cs).size ≤ _; rw [TreeCircuit.size_node]; omega
++  | _ + 1, [] => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.size_node, TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil]
++  | _ + 1, [c] => by show c.size ≤ _; rw [TreeCircuit.sumSize_cons, TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil]; omega
++  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
++      have ih := combineFuel_size b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))
++      have h2 := sumSize_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
++      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).size ≤ _
++      omega
++
++/-- The balanced tree computes the unbounded gate:
++`(TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.balanced_eval (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x :=
++  combineFuel_eval b x _ cs
++
++/-- The balanced tree has fan-in 2, unless a child already had more:
++`(TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs)`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_balanced_le (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    (TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs) :=
++  combineFuel_maxFanin b _ cs (le_refl _)
++
++/-- The balanced tree adds at most `⌈log₂ |cs|⌉ + 1` to the children's depth:
++`(TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).depth ≤ TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs + Nat.clog 2 cs.length + 1`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_balanced_le (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    (TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).depth ≤ TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs + Nat.clog 2 cs.length + 1 :=
++  combineFuel_depth b _ cs (le_refl _)
++
++/-- The balanced tree adds at most `|cs| + 1` to the children's total size:
++`(TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).size ≤ TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length + 1`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.size_balanced_le (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    (TreeCircuit.balanced b cs).size ≤ TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length + 1 :=
++  combineFuel_size b _ cs
++
++/-- The balanced tree over no children is the empty gate: `TreeCircuit.balanced b [] =
++TreeCircuit.node b []`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.balanced_nil (b : Bool) :
++    TreeCircuit.balanced b ([] : List (TreeCircuit n)) = TreeCircuit.node b [] := rfl
++
++/-- The balanced tree over one child is that child: `TreeCircuit.balanced b [c] = c`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.balanced_singleton (b : Bool) (c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.balanced b [c] = c := rfl
++
++end BoolCircuit

+-private def pairUp (b : Bool) : List (TreeCircuit n) → List (TreeCircuit n)

+-  | c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => TreeCircuit.node b [c₁, c₂] :: pairUp b cs
+-

+-private theorem eval_node_pairUp (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) :

+-
+-/-- Pairing adds at most one to the depth. -/
+-private theorem maxDepth_pairUp (b : Bool) :

+-      omega
+-
+-/-- Pairing introduces only fan-in-2 gates. -/
+-private theorem maxFaninL_pairUp (b : Bool) :

+-  | [] => Nat.zero_le _

+-          = max 2 (max c₁.maxFanin (max c₂.maxFanin 0)) := by

+-          TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
+-        norm_num
+-      simp only [pairUp, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, h1]
+-      omega
+-
+-/-- Repeatedly pair a child list, `k` rounds at most, into a single circuit. -/
+-private def combineFuel (b : Bool) : ℕ → List (TreeCircuit n) → TreeCircuit n
+-  | 0, cs => TreeCircuit.node b cs
+-  | _ + 1, [] => TreeCircuit.node b []
+-  | _ + 1, [c] => c
+-  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))
+-
+-/-- Combine a child list into a balanced binary tree of gates of type `b`. -/
+-private def combine (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) : TreeCircuit n :=
+-  combineFuel b cs.length cs
+-
+-/-- Combining computes the same value as the unbounded gate. -/
+-private theorem combineFuel_eval (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+-    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)),
+-      (combineFuel b k cs).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x
+-  | 0, _ => rfl
+-  | _ + 1, [] => rfl
+-  | _ + 1, [c] => by cases b <;> simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.eval]
+-  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
+-      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).eval x = _
+-      rw [combineFuel_eval b x k, eval_node_pairUp]
+-
+-/-- Combining produces only fan-in-2 gates, given enough rounds. -/
+-private theorem combineFuel_maxFanin (b : Bool) :
+-    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)), cs.length ≤ k →
+-      (combineFuel b k cs).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs)
+-  | 0, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
+-  | 0, _ :: _, h => by simp at h
+-  | _ + 1, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
+-  | _ + 1, [c], _ => by
+-      show c.maxFanin ≤ _
+-      rw [TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_cons, TreeCircuit.maxFaninL_nil]
+-      omega
+-  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs, h => by
+-      have hp := length_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
+-      have hlen : (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)).length ≤ k := by
+-        simp only [List.length_cons] at h hp ⊢; omega
+-      have ih := combineFuel_maxFanin b k _ hlen
+-      have h2 := maxFaninL_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
+-      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).maxFanin ≤ _
+-      omega
+-
+-/-- Combining `m` children costs `⌈log₂ m⌉` extra levels of depth.
+-
+-**Proof sketch.** Induction on the fuel.  With no children, or one child, the bound is
+-immediate.  With at least two children, one round of pairing halves the list (to
+-`⌈m / 2⌉` entries) and raises the maximum child depth by at most one, while
+-`⌈log₂ m⌉ = ⌈log₂ ⌈m / 2⌉⌉ + 1` for `m ≥ 2`; the induction hypothesis on the paired list
+-then gives the bound. -/
+-private theorem combineFuel_depth (b : Bool) :
+-    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)), cs.length ≤ k →
+-      (combineFuel b k cs).depth ≤ TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs + Nat.clog 2 cs.length + 1
+-  | 0, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.depth_node, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
+-  | 0, _ :: _, h => by simp at h
+-  | _ + 1, [], _ => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.depth_node, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
+-  | _ + 1, [c], _ => by
+-      show c.depth ≤ _
+-      rw [TreeCircuit.maxDepth_cons, TreeCircuit.maxDepth_nil]
+-      simp
+-  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs, h => by
+-      have hp := length_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
+-      have hlen : (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)).length ≤ k := by
+-        simp only [List.length_cons] at h hp ⊢; omega
+-      have ih := combineFuel_depth b k _ hlen

+-/-- Combining `m` children costs at most `m` extra gates. -/
+-private theorem combineFuel_size (b : Bool) :
+-    ∀ (k : ℕ) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)),
+-      (combineFuel b k cs).size ≤ TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length + 1
+-  | 0, cs => by show (TreeCircuit.node b cs).size ≤ _; rw [TreeCircuit.size_node]; omega
+-  | _ + 1, [] => by simp [combineFuel, TreeCircuit.size_node, TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil]
+-  | _ + 1, [c] => by show c.size ≤ _; rw [TreeCircuit.sumSize_cons, TreeCircuit.sumSize_nil]; omega
+-  | k + 1, c₁ :: c₂ :: cs => by
+-      have ih := combineFuel_size b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))
+-      have h2 := sumSize_pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)
+-      show (combineFuel b k (pairUp b (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs))).size ≤ _
+-      omega
+-
+-/-- `combine` computes the unbounded gate. -/
+-private theorem combine_eval (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+-    (combine b cs).eval x = (TreeCircuit.node b cs).eval x :=
+-  combineFuel_eval b x _ cs
+-
+-/-- `combine` has fan-in 2, unless a child already had more. -/
+-private theorem combine_maxFanin (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
+-    (combine b cs).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL cs) :=
+-  combineFuel_maxFanin b _ cs (le_refl _)
+-
+-/-- `combine` adds `⌈log₂ |cs|⌉ + 1` to the children's depth. -/
+-private theorem combine_depth (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
+-    (combine b cs).depth ≤ TreeCircuit.maxDepth cs + Nat.clog 2 cs.length + 1 :=
+-  combineFuel_depth b _ cs (le_refl _)
+-
+-/-- `combine` adds `|cs| + 1` to the children's total size. -/
+-private theorem combine_size (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
+-    (combine b cs).size ≤ TreeCircuit.sumSize cs + cs.length + 1 :=
+-  combineFuel_size b _ cs
+-
+ /-- Rebuild every gate of a circuit as a balanced binary tree of gates of the same
+ type, so that the result has fan-in `2`.  [AB09, p. 118] -/
+ def TreeCircuit.toBinary : TreeCircuit n → TreeCircuit n
+   | .lit l => .lit l
+-  | .node b cs => combine b (cs.map TreeCircuit.toBinary)
++  | .node b cs => TreeCircuit.balanced b (cs.map TreeCircuit.toBinary)
+

++/-!
++# Bounded-width branching programs
++
++Branching programs of bounded width in the general model of [Bar89, §1] (instructions produce
++maps `[w] → [w]`; permutation programs are a predicate), the host `BranchingProgram.evalWith`
++that runs a program on state vectors (registry row H18), and both directions of Barrington's
++results, namely formulas of depth `d` to width-5 permutation programs of length `≤ 4^d`, programs
++of length `l` to formulas of depth `O(log l)`, and BWBP = nonuniform NC¹ = PBP₅.
++
++## Contents
++
++- `BranchingPrograms.Defs`: instructions, programs, `yield` in program order, `IsPermutation`,
++  `conj`, `complementLast`, families with accepting sets, the classes `InBWBP` and `InPBP`.
++- `BranchingPrograms.Eval`: the push-form host `evalWith`, `evalWith_rel`, the Boolean semantics.
++- `BranchingPrograms.Barrington`: five-cycle recognition, Lemmas 1–3, the explicit conjugator and
++  the computable construction `barrington` (Thm 1).
++- `BranchingPrograms.BarringtonConverse`: the composition-tree formula `toFormula` (Thm 2).
++- `BranchingPrograms.NC1`: the `DAGCircuit` corollary, NC¹ ⊆ PBP₅, BWBP ⊆ NC¹ and the Corollary.
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++-/

++import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.Inv

++/-!
++# Barrington's Theorem 1: formulas to width-5 permutation programs
++
++[Bar89, Thm 1]: a fan-in-2 AND/OR formula of depth `d` over literals is *five-cycle recognized*
++by a width-5 permutation branching program of length at most `4^d`.  The construction
++`BranchingProgram.barrington` is computable (P6-Q13): Lemma 1's conjugator is the explicit
++`θ (σ^k 0) = τ^k 0`, and the OR case goes through Lemma 2 by De Morgan.
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BranchingProgram.IsFiveCycle`, `BranchingProgram.FiveCycleRecognizes` — five-cycles and
++  five-cycle recognition [Bar89, §3].
++* `BranchingProgram.sigma1`, `sigma2` — Lemma 3's five-cycles `(12345)`, `(13542)`, 0-based.
++* `BranchingProgram.conjugator` — Lemma 1's explicit conjugator (with `conjugatorIndex`,
++  `conjugatorFun`, `conjugatorTable`).
++* `BranchingProgram.commutatorProgram` — the AND step `B₁ B₂ B₁' B₂'` of Thm 1's proof.
++* `BranchingProgram.barrington` — Barrington's program for a formula and an output.
++
++## Main results
++
++All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
++
++* `isFiveCycle_sigma1`, `isFiveCycle_sigma2`, `isFiveCycle_commutator` — Lemma 3 (C9).
++* `conjugator_conj` — the conjugator conjugates (C10).
++* `FiveCycleRecognizes.conj`, `FiveCycleRecognizes.complementLast` — Lemmas 1 and 2 (C11, C12).
++* `barrington_recognizes`, `barrington_length_le`, `barrington_length_pos`,
++  `isPermutation_barrington`, `barrington_yield_zero` — Thm 1 (C13–C16).
++
++## Conventions and deviations
++
++* **Orders.** Instructions follow BV14's order (`map0` on `0`) and programs compose in program
++  order (`Defs.lean`).  Bar89's commutator `σ₁σ₂σ₁⁻¹σ₂⁻¹`, read left to right, is the Mathlib
++  product `sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1`, which is `(13254)` 0-based, i.e. the cycle
++  `0 ↦ 2 ↦ 1 ↦ 4 ↦ 3 ↦ 0`; the right-to-left reading gives `(14352)` instead (r3 §H3.6).
++* **Cycle notation.** `sigma1`, `sigma2` are written `List.formPerm [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]` and
++  `List.formPerm [0, 2, 4, 3, 1]`: Mathlib's `c[…]` notation lives in
++  `Mathlib.GroupTheory.Perm.Cycle.Concrete`, which is not in the pinned Mathlib cache;
++  `c[l]` is `Cycle.formPerm (Cycle.ofList l)`, which is `List.formPerm l` (`Cycle.formPerm_coe`).
++* **Constants and literals.** [Bar89, Thm 1] allows "inputs and negated inputs"; `TreeCircuit`
++  also has constant nodes `node true []`, `node false []` and fan-in-1 nodes, which get their own
++  trivial cases (the ingest's transcription note).  A constant reads variable `0`, hence
++  `[NeZero n]`.
++* **OR gates.** "Appeal to Lemma 2" is De Morgan through `complementLast` (see `barrington`).
++* **Fan-in.** `barrington` is total; at a node of fan-in above 2 it uses the first two children
++  only.  The theorems assume `c.maxFanin ≤ 2`, where nothing is dropped.
++* **Reading.** BV14 reads the state reached from the start state; here the start state is `0`
++  and the accepting reading is `σ 0` (C16).
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
++  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false
++
++namespace BranchingProgram
++
++open BoolCircuit Equiv
++
++variable {n : ℕ}
++
++/-! ### Five-cycles and five-cycle recognition -/
++
++/-- `σ` is a five-cycle: its cycle type is `{5}`, `σ.cycleType = {5}` (decidable)
++[Bar89, §3, p003-b001]. -/
++def IsFiveCycle (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) : Prop :=
++  σ.cycleType = {5}
++
++/-- Reader of `IsFiveCycle`: `IsFiveCycle σ ↔ σ.cycleType = {5}`. -/
++theorem isFiveCycle_iff (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) : IsFiveCycle σ ↔ σ.cycleType = {5} := Iff.rfl
++
++instance instDecidablePredIsFiveCycle : DecidablePred IsFiveCycle := fun σ =>
++  inferInstanceAs (Decidable (σ.cycleType = {5}))
++
++/-- `B` five-cycle recognizes `A` with output `σ`: `σ` is a five-cycle, and `B.yield x` is `σ` on
++`A` and the identity off `A`,
++`IsFiveCycle σ ∧ ∀ x, (A x → B.yield x = σ) ∧ (¬ A x → B.yield x = id)`
++[Bar89, §3, p003-b001]. -/
++def FiveCycleRecognizes (B : BranchingProgram 5 n) (A : (Fin n → Bool) → Prop)
++    (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) : Prop :=
++  IsFiveCycle σ ∧ ∀ x, (A x → B.yield x = σ) ∧ (¬ A x → B.yield x = id)
++
++/-- Reader of `FiveCycleRecognizes`: `FiveCycleRecognizes B A σ ↔
++IsFiveCycle σ ∧ ∀ x, (A x → B.yield x = σ) ∧ (¬ A x → B.yield x = id)`. -/
++theorem fiveCycleRecognizes_iff (B : BranchingProgram 5 n) (A : (Fin n → Bool) → Prop)
++    (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    FiveCycleRecognizes B A σ ↔
++      IsFiveCycle σ ∧ ∀ x, (A x → B.yield x = σ) ∧ (¬ A x → B.yield x = id) :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++/-! ### Lemma 3's five-cycles -/
++
++/-- Bar89's `(12345)`, 0-based: the five-cycle `0 ↦ 1 ↦ 2 ↦ 3 ↦ 4 ↦ 0`, Mathlib's `c[0,1,2,3,4]`,
++written `sigma1 = List.formPerm [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]` [Bar89, Lemma 3, p003-b005]. -/
++def sigma1 : Perm (Fin 5) :=
++  List.formPerm [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]
++
++/-- Reader of `sigma1`: `sigma1 = List.formPerm [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]`. -/
++theorem sigma1_eq : sigma1 = List.formPerm [0, 1, 2, 3, 4] := rfl
++
++/-- Bar89's `(13542)`, 0-based: the five-cycle `0 ↦ 2 ↦ 4 ↦ 3 ↦ 1 ↦ 0`, Mathlib's `c[0,2,4,3,1]`,
++written `sigma2 = List.formPerm [0, 2, 4, 3, 1]` [Bar89, Lemma 3, p003-b005]. -/
++def sigma2 : Perm (Fin 5) :=
++  List.formPerm [0, 2, 4, 3, 1]
++
++/-- Reader of `sigma2`: `sigma2 = List.formPerm [0, 2, 4, 3, 1]`. -/
++theorem sigma2_eq : sigma2 = List.formPerm [0, 2, 4, 3, 1] := rfl
++
++/-! ### Lemma 1's explicit conjugator -/
++
++/-- The position of `i` on the orbit of `0` under `σ`: the first `k < 5` with `(σ ^ k) 0 = i`, or
++`0` if there is none,
++`conjugatorIndex σ i = ((List.range 5).find? fun k => (σ ^ k) 0 = i).getD 0`.  Glue for
++[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003]; for a five-cycle every `i` is found. -/
++def conjugatorIndex (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) (i : Fin 5) : ℕ :=
++  ((List.range 5).find? fun k => (σ ^ k) 0 = i).getD 0
++
++/-- Reader of `conjugatorIndex`:
++`conjugatorIndex σ i = ((List.range 5).find? fun k => (σ ^ k) 0 = i).getD 0`. -/
++theorem conjugatorIndex_eq (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) (i : Fin 5) :
++    conjugatorIndex σ i = ((List.range 5).find? fun k => (σ ^ k) 0 = i).getD 0 := rfl
++
++/-- The conjugating map `σ^k 0 ↦ τ^k 0`: `conjugatorFun σ τ i = (τ ^ conjugatorIndex σ i) 0`
++[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003]. -/
++def conjugatorFun (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) (i : Fin 5) : Fin 5 :=
++  (τ ^ conjugatorIndex σ i) 0
++
++/-- Reader of `conjugatorFun`: `conjugatorFun σ τ i = (τ ^ conjugatorIndex σ i) 0`. -/
++theorem conjugatorFun_eq (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) (i : Fin 5) :
++    conjugatorFun σ τ i = (τ ^ conjugatorIndex σ i) 0 := rfl
++
++/-- The values of `conjugatorFun σ τ` stored as a list:
++`conjugatorTable σ τ = List.ofFn (conjugatorFun σ τ) = [f 0, f 1, f 2, f 3, f 4]` with
++`f = conjugatorFun σ τ`.  `conjugator` reads its map from this list (`getD_conjugatorTable`), so
++that evaluating a conjugator computes the five values once and later applications are lookups;
++without it, Barrington's nested conjugates cost time exponential in the depth when evaluated.
++Glue for [Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003]. -/
++def conjugatorTable (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) : List (Fin 5) :=
++  List.ofFn (conjugatorFun σ τ)
++
++/-- Reader of `conjugatorTable`: `conjugatorTable σ τ = List.ofFn (conjugatorFun σ τ)`. -/
++theorem conjugatorTable_eq (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    conjugatorTable σ τ = List.ofFn (conjugatorFun σ τ) := rfl
++
++/-- Looking up the table gives the map: `(conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0 = conjugatorFun σ τ i`. -/
++theorem getD_conjugatorTable (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) (i : Fin 5) :
++    (conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0 = conjugatorFun σ τ i := by
++  rw [conjugatorTable_eq, List.getD_eq_getElem?_getD, List.getElem?_ofFn]
++  simp
++
++/-- The explicit conjugator of two five-cycles: the permutation `θ` with `θ (σ^k 0) = τ^k 0`
++for `k < 5`, i.e. `conjugatorFun σ τ` (read from the list `conjugatorTable σ τ`) packaged with
++its computable inverse (`Fintype.bijInv`) when it is a bijection, and the identity otherwise
++[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003: "there exists some permutation `θ` with `τ = θσθ⁻¹`"].
++Computable (P6-Q13); it is not `Equiv.ofBijective`, whose inverse is noncomputable.  For
++five-cycles `σ`, `τ` the map is a bijection and `θ * σ * θ⁻¹ = τ` (C10). -/
++def conjugator (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) : Perm (Fin 5) :=
++  let t := conjugatorTable σ τ
++  if h : Function.Bijective (fun i : Fin 5 => t.getD i 0) then
++    { toFun := fun i => t.getD i 0
++      invFun := Fintype.bijInv h
++      left_inv := Fintype.leftInverse_bijInv h
++      right_inv := Fintype.rightInverse_bijInv h }
++  else 1
++
++/-- The looked-up map is `conjugatorFun σ τ`:
++`(fun i => (conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0) = conjugatorFun σ τ`.  Glue. -/
++private theorem getD_conjugatorTable_fun (σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    (fun i : Fin 5 => (conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0) = conjugatorFun σ τ :=
++  funext (getD_conjugatorTable σ τ)
++
++/-- Reader of `conjugator`, bijective case: if `conjugatorFun σ τ` is a bijection then
++`conjugator σ τ i = conjugatorFun σ τ i`. -/
++theorem conjugator_apply_of_bijective {σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)}
++    (h : Function.Bijective (conjugatorFun σ τ)) (i : Fin 5) :
++    conjugator σ τ i = conjugatorFun σ τ i := by
++  have h' : Function.Bijective (fun i : Fin 5 => (conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0) := by
++    rw [getD_conjugatorTable_fun]; exact h
++  unfold conjugator
++  rw [dif_pos h']
++  exact getD_conjugatorTable σ τ i
++
++/-- Reader of `conjugator`, degenerate case: if `conjugatorFun σ τ` is not a bijection then
++`conjugator σ τ = 1`. -/
++theorem conjugator_of_not_bijective {σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)}
++    (h : ¬ Function.Bijective (conjugatorFun σ τ)) : conjugator σ τ = 1 := by
++  have h' : ¬ Function.Bijective (fun i : Fin 5 => (conjugatorTable σ τ).getD i 0) := by
++    rw [getD_conjugatorTable_fun]; exact h
++  unfold conjugator
++  rw [dif_neg h']
++
++/-! ### Barrington's construction -/
++
++/-- The AND step of [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002] for output `σ`: with
++`θ = conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ` and `τᵢ = θ * sigmaᵢ * θ⁻¹`,
++`commutatorProgram P₁ P₂ σ = P₁ τ₁ ++ P₂ τ₂ ++ P₁ τ₁⁻¹ ++ P₂ τ₂⁻¹`, Bar89's `B₁B₂B₁'B₂'`.
++If `P₁ ρ`, `P₂ ρ` recognize `A₁`, `A₂` with output `ρ`, it yields `τ₂⁻¹ * τ₁⁻¹ * τ₂ * τ₁ =
++θ * (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) * θ⁻¹ = σ` on `A₁ ∩ A₂` and the identity elsewhere.
++Deviation: Bar89 takes `B₁, B₂` with outputs `σ₁, σ₂` and conjugates the result (Lemma 1); here
++the outputs `τᵢ` are conjugated in advance, which gives output `σ` directly. -/
++def commutatorProgram (P₁ P₂ : Perm (Fin 5) → BranchingProgram 5 n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    BranchingProgram 5 n :=
++  let θ := conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ
++  P₁ (θ * sigma1 * θ⁻¹) ++ P₂ (θ * sigma2 * θ⁻¹) ++ P₁ (θ * sigma1 * θ⁻¹)⁻¹ ++
++    P₂ (θ * sigma2 * θ⁻¹)⁻¹
++
++/-- Reader of `commutatorProgram`: with `θ = conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ`,
++`commutatorProgram P₁ P₂ σ = P₁ (θ * sigma1 * θ⁻¹) ++ P₂ (θ * sigma2 * θ⁻¹) ++
++P₁ (θ * sigma1 * θ⁻¹)⁻¹ ++ P₂ (θ * sigma2 * θ⁻¹)⁻¹`. -/
++theorem commutatorProgram_eq (P₁ P₂ : Perm (Fin 5) → BranchingProgram 5 n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    commutatorProgram P₁ P₂ σ =
++      P₁ (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ * sigma1 *
++          (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ)⁻¹) ++
++        P₂ (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ * sigma2 *
++          (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ)⁻¹) ++
++        P₁ (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ * sigma1 *
++          (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ)⁻¹)⁻¹ ++
++        P₂ (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ * sigma2 *
++          (conjugator (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) σ)⁻¹)⁻¹ := rfl
++
++/-- Barrington's program for a formula `c` with output `σ` [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002;
++BV14, Thm 3.5, p014-b001], by recursion on `c` with the output generalized:
++- the literal `x_i` gives `⟨i, 1, σ⟩` and `¬x_i` gives `⟨i, σ, 1⟩` (BV14's order: `map0` first);
++- the constant `node true []` (true) gives `⟨0, σ, σ⟩`, and `node false []` (false) gives
++  `⟨0, 1, 1⟩`; both read variable `0`, hence `[NeZero n]`;
++- a fan-in-1 node gives its child's program;
++- `node true (c₁ :: c₂ :: _)` (`∧`) gives `commutatorProgram (barrington c₁) (barrington c₂) σ`:
++  the children with outputs `τ₁, τ₂, τ₁⁻¹, τ₂⁻¹`, `τᵢ = θ * sigmaᵢ * θ⁻¹`, where `θ` conjugates the
++  commutator `sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1` to `σ`;
++- `node false (c₁ :: c₂ :: _)` (`∨`) is De Morgan through Lemma 2 (`complementLast`).
++  *Derivation.* If `B` recognizes `A` with output `ρ`, then `B.complementLast ρ` yields
++  `ρ⁻¹ ∘ B(x)`: the identity on `A` and `ρ⁻¹` off it, so it recognizes `¬A` with output `ρ⁻¹`
++  (C12).  Hence `N c μ := (barrington c μ⁻¹).complementLast μ⁻¹` recognizes `¬c` with output `μ`.
++  The AND step on `N c₁`, `N c₂` with output `σ⁻¹` recognizes `¬c₁ ∧ ¬c₂` with output `σ⁻¹`;
++  applying `complementLast σ⁻¹` gives `¬(¬c₁ ∧ ¬c₂) = c₁ ∨ c₂` with output `(σ⁻¹)⁻¹ = σ`.  Lemma 2
++  keeps lengths, so the length stays `4 · 4^(d−1) = 4^d`.
++Computable (P6-Q13); defined by well-founded recursion on `sizeOf c`, so it is evaluated by
++`#eval`, not by the kernel.  At a node of fan-in above 2 only the first two children are used:
++the theorems assume `c.maxFanin ≤ 2`, where nothing is dropped. -/
++def barrington [NeZero n] : TreeCircuit n → Perm (Fin 5) → BranchingProgram 5 n
++  | .lit ⟨i, true⟩, σ => ⟨[Instr.ofPerm i 1 σ]⟩
++  | .lit ⟨i, false⟩, σ => ⟨[Instr.ofPerm i σ 1]⟩
++  | .node true [], σ => ⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 σ σ]⟩
++  | .node false [], _ => ⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 1 1]⟩
++  | .node _ [c], σ => barrington c σ
++  | .node true (c₁ :: c₂ :: _), σ =>
++      commutatorProgram (fun ρ => barrington c₁ ρ) (fun ρ => barrington c₂ ρ) σ
++  | .node false (c₁ :: c₂ :: _), σ =>
++      (commutatorProgram (fun ρ => (barrington c₁ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹)
++        (fun ρ => (barrington c₂ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹) σ⁻¹).complementLast σ⁻¹
++termination_by c => sizeOf c
++
++section Readers
++
++variable [NeZero n]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, positive literal: `barrington (.lit ⟨i, true⟩) σ =
++⟨[Instr.ofPerm i 1 σ]⟩`. -/
++theorem barrington_lit_true (i : Fin n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.lit ⟨i, true⟩) σ = ⟨[Instr.ofPerm i 1 σ]⟩ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, negative literal: `barrington (.lit ⟨i, false⟩) σ =
++⟨[Instr.ofPerm i σ 1]⟩`. -/
++theorem barrington_lit_false (i : Fin n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.lit ⟨i, false⟩) σ = ⟨[Instr.ofPerm i σ 1]⟩ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, the constant true: `barrington (.node true []) σ =
++⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 σ σ]⟩`. -/
++theorem barrington_node_true_nil (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.node true [] : TreeCircuit n) σ = ⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 σ σ]⟩ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, the constant false: `barrington (.node false []) σ =
++⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 1 1]⟩`. -/
++theorem barrington_node_false_nil (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.node false [] : TreeCircuit n) σ = ⟨[Instr.ofPerm 0 1 1]⟩ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, fan-in 1: `barrington (.node b [c]) σ = barrington c σ`. -/
++theorem barrington_node_singleton (b : Bool) (c : TreeCircuit n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.node b [c]) σ = barrington c σ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, `∧` node: `barrington (.node true (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)) σ =
++commutatorProgram (fun ρ => barrington c₁ ρ) (fun ρ => barrington c₂ ρ) σ`. -/
++theorem barrington_node_true_cons_cons (c₁ c₂ : TreeCircuit n) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n))
++    (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.node true (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)) σ =
++      commutatorProgram (fun ρ => barrington c₁ ρ) (fun ρ => barrington c₂ ρ) σ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++/-- Reader of `barrington`, `∨` node: `barrington (.node false (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)) σ =
++(commutatorProgram (fun ρ => (barrington c₁ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹)
++(fun ρ => (barrington c₂ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹) σ⁻¹).complementLast σ⁻¹`. -/
++theorem barrington_node_false_cons_cons (c₁ c₂ : TreeCircuit n) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n))
++    (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    barrington (.node false (c₁ :: c₂ :: cs)) σ =
++      (commutatorProgram (fun ρ => (barrington c₁ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹)
++        (fun ρ => (barrington c₂ ρ⁻¹).complementLast ρ⁻¹) σ⁻¹).complementLast σ⁻¹ := by
++  rw [barrington]
++
++end Readers
++
++/-! ### Targets C9–C16 -/
++
++/-- **C9.** Lemma 3, first five-cycle: `IsFiveCycle sigma1` (Bar89's `(12345)`)
++[Bar89, Lemma 3, p003-b005].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Kernel `decide` (`isFiveCycle_iff`, `sigma1_eq`; no `native_decide`). -/
++theorem isFiveCycle_sigma1 : IsFiveCycle sigma1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C9.** Lemma 3, second five-cycle: `IsFiveCycle sigma2` (Bar89's `(13542)`)
++[Bar89, Lemma 3, p003-b005].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Kernel `decide` (`isFiveCycle_iff`, `sigma2_eq`; no `native_decide`). -/
++theorem isFiveCycle_sigma2 : IsFiveCycle sigma2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C9.** Lemma 3 in program order: the commutator of the two five-cycles is a five-cycle,
++`IsFiveCycle (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1)`; the product is `(13254)` 0-based,
++`List.formPerm [0, 2, 1, 4, 3]` [Bar89, Lemma 3, p003-b005: "`(12345)(13542)(54321)(24531) =
++(13254)`"].
++Deviation: Bar89's product is read left to right; the Mathlib product applies its right factor
++first, so Bar89's `σ₁σ₂σ₁⁻¹σ₂⁻¹` is `sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1` (r3 §H3.6).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Kernel `decide` (`isFiveCycle_iff`, `sigma1_eq`, `sigma2_eq`), as
++`decide +kernel`: the elaborator's pre-evaluation of plain `decide` hits `maxRecDepth` here
++(probe P2). -/
++theorem isFiveCycle_commutator : IsFiveCycle (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C10.** The explicit conjugator conjugates one five-cycle to the other:
++`IsFiveCycle σ → IsFiveCycle τ → conjugator σ τ * σ * (conjugator σ τ)⁻¹ = τ`
++[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003].  Checked by computation for all 24 × 24 pairs (r3 §H3.6).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Reduce to a finite check: every five-cycle is one of the 24 explicit
++`List.formPerm [0, a, b, c, d]` (`∀ σ, IsFiveCycle σ → σ ∈ l`, kernel `decide +kernel`, about
++15 s), and C10 holds on the 24 × 24 explicit pairs (kernel `decide +kernel`, about 40 s and
++5.5 GB; probes P3, P3b).  Alternatively argue: for a five-cycle `σ`, `k ↦ (σ ^ k) 0` is a
++bijection from `range 5` onto `Fin 5` (`Equiv.Perm.IsCycle`, `cycleType` `{5}`), so
++`conjugatorIndex σ (σ^k 0) = k`
++(`conjugatorIndex_eq`, `List.find?`), `conjugatorFun σ τ (σ^k 0) = τ^k 0` (`conjugatorFun_eq`)
++and it is a bijection; by `conjugator_apply_of_bijective`, `θ (σ (σ^k 0)) = τ^(k+1) 0 =
++τ (θ (σ^k 0))`, i.e. `θ * σ = τ * θ` on the orbit of `0`, which is everything. -/
++theorem conjugator_conj {σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)} (hσ : IsFiveCycle σ) (hτ : IsFiveCycle τ) :
++    conjugator σ τ * σ * (conjugator σ τ)⁻¹ = τ := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C11.** **Lemma 1**: change the output to any five-cycle at the same length: if `B` five-cycle
++recognizes `A` with output `σ` and `τ` is a five-cycle, then `B.conj (conjugator σ τ)` five-cycle
++recognizes `A` with output `τ` [Bar89, Lemma 1, p003-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `fiveCycleRecognizes_iff`; the yield of the conjugate is `θ ∘ B.yield x ∘ θ⁻¹`
++(`yield_conj`), which is `θ * σ * θ⁻¹ = τ` on `A` (`conjugator_conj`) and `θ ∘ θ⁻¹ = id` off `A`
++(`Equiv.self_comp_symm`).  The length is kept by `length_conj`. -/
++theorem FiveCycleRecognizes.conj {B : BranchingProgram 5 n} {A : (Fin n → Bool) → Prop}
++    {σ τ : Perm (Fin 5)} (h : FiveCycleRecognizes B A σ) (hτ : IsFiveCycle τ) :
++    FiveCycleRecognizes (B.conj (conjugator σ τ)) A τ := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C12.** **Lemma 2**: the complement, at the same length, with output `σ⁻¹`: if `B` (nonempty)
++five-cycle recognizes `A` with output `σ`, then `B.complementLast σ` five-cycle recognizes
++`fun x => ¬ A x` with output `σ⁻¹` [Bar89, Lemma 2, p003-b004].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `fiveCycleRecognizes_iff` and `yield_complementLast` (using `hB`): the new
++yield is `σ⁻¹ ∘ B.yield x`, which is `σ⁻¹ ∘ σ = id` on `A` (`Equiv.symm_comp_self`) and `σ⁻¹` off
++`A`; `σ⁻¹` is a five-cycle (`Equiv.Perm.cycleType_inv`). -/
++theorem FiveCycleRecognizes.complementLast {B : BranchingProgram 5 n}
++    {A : (Fin n → Bool) → Prop} {σ : Perm (Fin 5)} (h : FiveCycleRecognizes B A σ)
++    (hB : 0 < B.length) :
++    FiveCycleRecognizes (B.complementLast σ) (fun x => ¬ A x) σ⁻¹ := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C13.** **Barrington's Theorem 1**, recognition: for a fan-in-2 formula `c` and a five-cycle
++`σ`, `barrington c σ` five-cycle recognizes `{x | c.eval x = true}` with output `σ`
++[Bar89, Thm 1, p003-b002, proof p004-b002; BV14, Thm 3.5, p014-b001].
++Deviations: formulas over literals with constant and fan-in-1 nodes (each handled directly); the
++OR case by De Morgan through Lemma 2; BV14 states the theorem for NAND circuits.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`), generalizing `σ`; `maxFanin_node` and
++`maxFanin_le_maxFaninL` give each child fan-in `≤ 2` and the child list length `≤ 2`.
++* Literals and constants: one instruction (`barrington_lit_true`, …, `yield_eq`,
++  `Instr.eval_eq`, `Instr.ofPerm_map0`/`_map1`); the values by `TreeCircuit.eval_lit`
++  and `eval_node_true_iff`/`eval_node_false_iff`.
++* Fan-in 1: `barrington_node_singleton` and `eval_node_true`/`eval_node_false` on `[c]`.
++* `∧`: `barrington_node_true_cons_cons`, `commutatorProgram_eq`, `yield_append` (three times).
++  The outputs `τᵢ` are five-cycles (`Equiv.Perm.cycleType_conj`, `cycleType_inv`), so the
++  induction hypothesis applies to each block; on `c₁ ∧ c₂` the yield is
++  `θ * (sigma2⁻¹ * sigma1⁻¹ * sigma2 * sigma1) * θ⁻¹ = σ` (`isFiveCycle_commutator`,
++  `conjugator_conj`), and off it two blocks cancel.
++* `∨`: `barrington_node_false_cons_cons`; C12 (with `barrington_length_pos`) turns each child
++  into a recognizer of its negation, the `∧` case gives `¬c₁ ∧ ¬c₂` with output `σ⁻¹`, and C12
++  again gives `c₁ ∨ c₂` with output `σ` (`inv_inv`). -/
++theorem barrington_recognizes [NeZero n] (c : TreeCircuit n) (hc : c.maxFanin ≤ 2)
++    {σ : Perm (Fin 5)} (hσ : IsFiveCycle σ) :
++    FiveCycleRecognizes (barrington c σ) (fun x => c.eval x = true) σ := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C14.** **Theorem 1**, length: `(barrington c σ).length ≤ 4 ^ c.depth` for a fan-in-2
++formula [Bar89, Thm 1, p003-b002: "of length at most `4^d`"].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`) generalizing `σ`.  Leaves have length 1
++and depth 0 or 1 (`depth_node`, `maxDepth_nil`).  Fan-in 1 keeps the length and adds one level.
++`∧`: four blocks (`commutatorProgram_eq`, `length_append`) each of length `≤ 4 ^ maxDepth`
++(`depth_le_maxDepth`), so `≤ 4 · 4 ^ maxDepth = 4 ^ (1 + maxDepth) = 4 ^ depth` (`depth_node`).
++`∨`: the same, `complementLast` keeping lengths (`length_complementLast`). -/
++theorem barrington_length_le [NeZero n] {c : TreeCircuit n} (hc : c.maxFanin ≤ 2)
++    (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) : (barrington c σ).length ≤ 4 ^ c.depth := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C14.** Every Barrington program is nonempty: `0 < (barrington c σ).length`.  Glue for
++Lemma 2 (`yield_complementLast` needs a last instruction).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`) generalizing `σ`, by the readers
++`barrington_lit_true`, …: leaves have one instruction; fan-in 1 is the child; the `∧` and `∨`
++programs contain a child's program (`commutatorProgram_eq`, `length_append`,
++`length_complementLast`). -/
++theorem barrington_length_pos [NeZero n] (c : TreeCircuit n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    0 < (barrington c σ).length := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C15.** Every Barrington program is a permutation program: `(barrington c σ).IsPermutation`.
++Glue; [Bar89, Thm 1, p003-b002]: "a 5-PBP".
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`) generalizing `σ`: leaves are
++`Instr.ofPerm` instructions (`Instr.isPermutation_ofPerm`, `isPermutation_iff`); the `∧` and `∨`
++programs are closed under `IsPermutation.append` and `IsPermutation.complementLast`
++(`commutatorProgram_eq`). -/
++theorem isPermutation_barrington [NeZero n] (c : TreeCircuit n) (σ : Perm (Fin 5)) :
++    (barrington c σ).IsPermutation := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C16.** BV14's reading from start state `0`: for a fan-in-2 formula and a five-cycle `σ`,
++`(barrington c σ).yield x 0 = if c.eval x then σ 0 else 0` [BV14, Thm 3.5, p014-b001; §3.3,
++p013-b002].
++Deviation: BV14 reads coordinate `1` of a 1-based state; here the start state is `0` and an
++accepted input ends at `σ 0 ≠ 0` (r3 §H3.7).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `barrington_recognizes` and `fiveCycleRecognizes_iff`; split on `c.eval x`. -/
++theorem barrington_yield_zero [NeZero n] {c : TreeCircuit n} (hc : c.maxFanin ≤ 2)
++    {σ : Perm (Fin 5)} (hσ : IsFiveCycle σ) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (barrington c σ).yield x 0 = if c.eval x then σ 0 else 0 := by
++  sorry
++
++end BranchingProgram

++import Mathlib.Data.Finset.Dedup
++import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.Defs
++import Mathlib.Data.List.FinRange
++import TCSlib.Complexity.BranchingPrograms.Defs
++import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.FormulaArith.Combinators

++/-!
++# Barrington's Theorem 2: branching programs to logarithmic-depth formulas
++
++[Bar89, Thm 2]: a width-`w` branching program of length `l` is recognized by a fan-in-2 circuit
++of depth `O(log l)`, the constant depending on `w`.  The circuit is built here as a fan-in-2
++formula (`BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit`, P6-Q18) that mirrors the three sections of Bar89's proof:
++
++1. *a constant-depth section per instruction* (`Instr.wire`): a map `f : [w] → [w]` is
++   represented by the `w²` wires "`f(i) = j`", and for one instruction each wire is `true`,
++   `false`, `x_var` or `¬x_var`;
++2. *a binary tree of composition circuits* (`wireComp`, `compTree`): the wire
++   `(g ∘ f)(i) = k` is `⋁_j (f(i) = j ∧ g(j) = k)`, a balanced fan-in-2 `∨`
++   (`BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit.balanced`); the instruction list is split at its midpoint;
++3. *a constant-depth acceptance section* (`toFormula`): `⋁_{f ∈ F} ⋀_i (B(x)(i) = f(i))`.
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BranchingProgram.Instr.wire`, `wireComp`, `compTree` — the sections 1 and 2.
++* `BranchingProgram.toFormula` — the whole formula (`noncomputable`: it enumerates `F`).
++* `BranchingProgram.topDepth` — the depth constant of the acceptance section.
++
++## Main results
++
++All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
++
++* `Instr.wire_eval`, `Instr.depth_wire_le`, `Instr.maxFanin_wire_le` (C17).
++* `wireComp_eval`, `depth_wireComp_le` (C18).
++* `compTree_eval`, `depth_compTree_le`, `maxFanin_compTree_le` (C19).
++* `toFormula_eval` (C20), `depth_toFormula_le`, `maxFanin_toFormula_le` (C21).
++
++## Deviations
++
++* Bar89 builds a circuit; here it is a formula (fan-out one), of size `l^{O(log w)}`, polynomial
++  for fixed `w` (`TreeCircuit.size_succ_le_two_pow`), as ruled by P6-Q18.  It reaches the
++  `DAGCircuit` classes through `Language.InTreeNC.inNC` (`NC1.lean`).
++* The depth constants, left implicit by Bar89, are explicit: `(⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)·⌈log₂ l⌉ + 1` for the
++  composition tree and `topDepth w = w·⌈log₂ w⌉ + ⌈log₂ w⌉ + 3` on top.
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false
++
++namespace BranchingProgram
++
++open BoolCircuit
++
++variable {w n : ℕ}
++
++/-! ### The constant-depth section per instruction -/
++
++/-- The wire "`ι` sends `i` to `j`" as a formula of depth at most 1: `true` (`node true []`) if
++both maps send `i` to `j`, the literal `¬x_var` if only `map0` does, `x_var` if only `map1` does,
++and `false` (`node false []`) if neither,
++`ι.wire i j = if ι.map0 i = j then (if ι.map1 i = j then node true [] else lit ⟨ι.var, false⟩)
++else (if ι.map1 i = j then lit ⟨ι.var, true⟩ else node false [])`
++[Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003: "a constant-depth section to find the function yielded by a each
++instruction", sic]. -/
++def Instr.wire (ι : Instr w n) (i j : Fin w) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  if ι.map0 i = j then
++    if ι.map1 i = j then .node true [] else .lit ⟨ι.var, false⟩
++  else
++    if ι.map1 i = j then .lit ⟨ι.var, true⟩ else .node false []
++
++/-- Reader of `Instr.wire`: `ι.wire i j = if ι.map0 i = j then (if ι.map1 i = j then
++node true [] else lit ⟨ι.var, false⟩) else (if ι.map1 i = j then lit ⟨ι.var, true⟩ else
++node false [])`. -/
++theorem Instr.wire_eq (ι : Instr w n) (i j : Fin w) :
++    ι.wire i j =
++      if ι.map0 i = j then
++        (if ι.map1 i = j then .node true [] else .lit ⟨ι.var, false⟩)
++      else
++        (if ι.map1 i = j then .lit ⟨ι.var, true⟩ else .node false []) := rfl
++
++/-! ### Composition circuits and the composition tree -/
++
++/-- One composition circuit: from the wires `F i j` ("`f(i) = j`") and `G j k` ("`g(j) = k`"),
++the wire "`(g ∘ f)(i) = k`" is the balanced fan-in-2 disjunction
++`wireComp F G i k = balanced false [node true [F i j, G j k] | j ∈ finRange w]`
++[Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003: "may be computed by a fixed circuit whose size depends only on
++`w`"]. -/
++def wireComp (F G : Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n) : Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n :=
++  fun i k => TreeCircuit.balanced false ((List.finRange w).map fun j => .node true [F i j, G j k])
++
++/-- Reader of `wireComp`:
++`wireComp F G i k = balanced false ((finRange w).map fun j => node true [F i j, G j k])`. -/
++theorem wireComp_eq (F G : Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n) (i k : Fin w) :
++    wireComp F G i k =
++      TreeCircuit.balanced false ((List.finRange w).map fun j => .node true [F i j, G j k]) :=
++  rfl
++
++/-- The binary tree of composition circuits over an instruction list, split at the midpoint: the
++empty list gives the identity wires (`node true []` on the diagonal, `node false []` off it), one
++instruction gives its wires, and a list `l` of length at least 2 gives
++`wireComp (compTree (l.take (|l| / 2))) (compTree (l.drop (|l| / 2)))` (the first half acts
++first) [Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003: "a binary tree of composition circuits"].  Defined by
++well-founded recursion on the length. -/
++def compTree : List (Instr w n) → Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n
++  | [] => fun i j => if i = j then .node true [] else .node false []
++  | [ι] => ι.wire
++  | ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l =>
++      wireComp (compTree ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).take ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).length / 2)))
++        (compTree ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).drop ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).length / 2)))
++termination_by l => l.length
++decreasing_by
++  all_goals simp only [List.length_take, List.length_drop, List.length_cons]
++  all_goals omega
++
++/-- Reader of `compTree`, empty list: `compTree [] i j = if i = j then node true [] else
++node false []`. -/
++theorem compTree_nil (i j : Fin w) :
++    compTree ([] : List (Instr w n)) i j =
++      if i = j then .node true [] else .node false [] := by
++  rw [compTree]
++
++/-- Reader of `compTree`, one instruction: `compTree [ι] = ι.wire`. -/
++theorem compTree_singleton (ι : Instr w n) : compTree [ι] = ι.wire := by
++  rw [compTree]
++
++/-- Reader of `compTree`, at least two instructions: with `l' = ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l`,
++`compTree l' = wireComp (compTree (l'.take (l'.length / 2))) (compTree (l'.drop (l'.length / 2)))`.
++-/
++theorem compTree_cons_cons (ι₁ ι₂ : Instr w n) (l : List (Instr w n)) :
++    compTree (ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l) =
++      wireComp (compTree ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).take ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).length / 2)))
++        (compTree ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).drop ((ι₁ :: ι₂ :: l).length / 2))) := by
++  rw [compTree]
++
++/-! ### The acceptance section -/
++
++/-- **Barrington's Theorem 2, one program** [Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003]: the formula
++`⋁_{f ∈ F} ⋀_i compTree B.instrs i (f i)` (the constant-depth acceptance section on top of the
++composition tree), `B.toFormula F = balanced false [balanced true [compTree B.instrs i (f i) |
++i ∈ finRange w] | f ∈ F.toList]`; it decides `B.yield x ∈ F` (C20).
++Deviation: built as a formula (fan-out one), not a DAG (P6-Q18); `noncomputable`, because it
++enumerates the `Finset` `F` (`Finset.toList`). -/
++noncomputable def toFormula (B : BranchingProgram w n) (F : Finset (Fin w → Fin w)) :
++    TreeCircuit n :=
++  TreeCircuit.balanced false (F.toList.map fun f =>
++    TreeCircuit.balanced true ((List.finRange w).map fun i => compTree B.instrs i (f i)))
++
++/-- Reader of `toFormula`: `B.toFormula F = balanced false (F.toList.map fun f =>
++balanced true ((finRange w).map fun i => compTree B.instrs i (f i)))`. -/
++theorem toFormula_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (F : Finset (Fin w → Fin w)) :
++    B.toFormula F = TreeCircuit.balanced false (F.toList.map fun f =>
++      TreeCircuit.balanced true ((List.finRange w).map fun i => compTree B.instrs i (f i))) :=
++  rfl
++
++/-- The depth of the acceptance section for width `w`:
++`topDepth w = w * Nat.clog 2 w + Nat.clog 2 w + 3` (one level for the leaves of the composition
++tree, `⌈log₂ w⌉ + 1` for the `⋀_i`, `⌈log₂ |F|⌉ + 1 ≤ w·⌈log₂ w⌉ + 1` for the `⋁_f`, since
++`|F| ≤ w^w`) [Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003: "a constant-depth section at the top"]. -/
++def topDepth (w : ℕ) : ℕ :=
++  w * Nat.clog 2 w + Nat.clog 2 w + 3
++
++/-- Reader of `topDepth`: `topDepth w = w * Nat.clog 2 w + Nat.clog 2 w + 3`. -/
++theorem topDepth_eq (w : ℕ) : topDepth w = w * Nat.clog 2 w + Nat.clog 2 w + 3 := rfl
++
++/-! ### Targets C17–C21 -/
++
++/-- **C17.** The wire of one instruction is correct: `(ι.wire i j).eval x =
++decide (ι.eval x i = j)` [Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `Instr.wire_eq` and `Instr.eval_eq`; split on `ι.map0 i = j`, `ι.map1 i = j` and
++`x ι.var`.  The constants evaluate by `TreeCircuit.eval_node_true_iff`/`eval_node_false_iff` on
++`[]`, the literals by `TreeCircuit.eval_lit` and a reader of `Lit.eval` (missing at the base;
++proposed `Lit.eval_eq : l.eval x = if l.sign then x l.idx else !x l.idx`). -/
++theorem Instr.wire_eval (ι : Instr w n) (i j : Fin w) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (ι.wire i j).eval x = decide (ι.eval x i = j) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C17.** The wire of one instruction has depth at most 1: `(ι.wire i j).depth ≤ 1`
++[Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003: "constant-depth section"].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `Instr.wire_eq`; an empty gate has depth 1 (`TreeCircuit.depth_node`,
++`maxDepth_nil`) and a literal depth 0 (reader `TreeCircuit.depth_lit`, missing at the base and
++proposed). -/
++theorem Instr.depth_wire_le (ι : Instr w n) (i j : Fin w) : (ι.wire i j).depth ≤ 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C17.** The wire of one instruction has fan-in at most 2: `(ι.wire i j).maxFanin ≤ 2`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `Instr.wire_eq`; an empty gate has fan-in 0 (`TreeCircuit.maxFanin_node`,
++`maxFaninL_nil`) and a literal fan-in 0 (reader `TreeCircuit.maxFanin_lit`, missing at the base
++and proposed). -/
++theorem Instr.maxFanin_wire_le (ι : Instr w n) (i j : Fin w) : (ι.wire i j).maxFanin ≤ 2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C18.** One composition circuit composes: if the wires `F` represent `f x` and the wires `G`
++represent `g x`, then `(wireComp F G i k).eval x = decide (g x (f x i) = k)`
++[Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `wireComp_eq`, `TreeCircuit.balanced_eval`, `eval_node_false` (an `any` over
++`finRange w`) and `eval_node_true` on the pairs; by `hF`, `hG` the pair `j` is true iff
++`f x i = j ∧ g x j = k`, and exactly `j = f x i` can be (`List.mem_finRange`). -/
++theorem wireComp_eval {F G : Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n}
++    {f g : (Fin n → Bool) → Fin w → Fin w}
++    (hF : ∀ x i j, (F i j).eval x = decide (f x i = j))
++    (hG : ∀ x i j, (G i j).eval x = decide (g x i = j)) (i k : Fin w) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (wireComp F G i k).eval x = decide (g x (f x i) = k) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C18.** A composition circuit adds `⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2` levels: if all wires of `F` and `G` have
++depth `≤ d`, then `(wireComp F G i k).depth ≤ d + Nat.clog 2 w + 2` [Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `wireComp_eq` and `TreeCircuit.depth_balanced_le`: each `node true [F i j, G j k]`
++has depth `≤ d + 1` (`depth_node`, `maxDepth_cons`), the list has length `w`
++(`List.length_map`, `List.length_finRange`), so the depth is `≤ (d + 1) + ⌈log₂ w⌉ + 1`. -/
++theorem depth_wireComp_le {F G : Fin w → Fin w → TreeCircuit n} {d : ℕ}
++    (hF : ∀ i j, (F i j).depth ≤ d) (hG : ∀ i j, (G i j).depth ≤ d) (i k : Fin w) :
++    (wireComp F G i k).depth ≤ d + Nat.clog 2 w + 2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C19.** The composition tree computes the yield: `(compTree B.instrs i j).eval x =
++decide (B.yield x i = j)` [Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Strong induction on the length of an instruction list `l`, for the yield of
++`⟨l⟩`: the empty list by `compTree_nil` and `yield_nil`; one instruction by `compTree_singleton`,
++`Instr.wire_eval` and `yield_eq`; otherwise `compTree_cons_cons`, `wireComp_eval` with the
++induction hypothesis for both halves, and `yield_append` on `l = l.take m ++ l.drop m`
++(`List.take_append_drop`, `append_instrs`). -/
++theorem compTree_eval (B : BranchingProgram w n) (i j : Fin w) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (compTree B.instrs i j).eval x = decide (B.yield x i = j) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C19.** The composition tree has depth `O(log l)`:
++`(compTree B.instrs i j).depth ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * Nat.clog 2 B.length + 1`
++[Bar89, Thm 2 proof, p004-b003].
++Constant (fixed at the statement phase): the leaves have depth `≤ 1` (identity wires and
++`Instr.wire`), and a list of length `l ≥ 2` splits into halves of lengths `⌊l/2⌋ ≤ ⌈l/2⌉` with
++`⌈log₂ ⌈l/2⌉⌉ = ⌈log₂ l⌉ − 1`, each split adding `⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2` levels (C18).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Strong induction on the length `l` of the instruction list (with `length_eq`):
++`compTree_nil`, `compTree_singleton` with `Instr.depth_wire_le` give depth `≤ 1`; for `l ≥ 2`,
++`compTree_cons_cons` and `depth_wireComp_le` with the induction hypothesis for both halves
++(`List.length_take`, `List.length_drop`), `Nat.clog_mono_right` and
++`Nat.clog_of_two_le` (`⌈log₂ l⌉ = ⌈log₂ ⌈l/2⌉⌉ + 1`). -/
++theorem depth_compTree_le (B : BranchingProgram w n) (i j : Fin w) :
++    (compTree B.instrs i j).depth ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * Nat.clog 2 B.length + 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C19.** The composition tree has fan-in at most 2: `(compTree B.instrs i j).maxFanin ≤ 2`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Strong induction on the length as for `compTree_eval`: the leaves by
++`compTree_nil` (`maxFanin_node`) and `Instr.maxFanin_wire_le`; a composition circuit by
++`wireComp_eq`, `TreeCircuit.maxFanin_balanced_le` and `maxFanin_node` on the binary `∧` nodes. -/
++theorem maxFanin_compTree_le (B : BranchingProgram w n) (i j : Fin w) :
++    (compTree B.instrs i j).maxFanin ≤ 2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C20.** Barrington's Theorem 2 formula decides acceptance:
++`(B.toFormula F).eval x = decide (B.yield x ∈ F)` [Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `toFormula_eq`, `TreeCircuit.balanced_eval`, `eval_node_false` (an `any` over
++`F.toList`, `Finset.mem_toList`) and `eval_node_true` (an `all` over `finRange w`); by
++`compTree_eval` the conjunction for `f` holds iff `∀ i, B.yield x i = f i`, i.e.
++`B.yield x = f` (`funext`). -/
++theorem toFormula_eval (B : BranchingProgram w n) (F : Finset (Fin w → Fin w))
++    (x : Fin n → Bool) : (B.toFormula F).eval x = decide (B.yield x ∈ F) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C21.** Depth `O(log l)` with a constant depending on `w`:
++`(B.toFormula F).depth ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * Nat.clog 2 B.length + topDepth w`
++[Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003: "depth `O(log l)`, where the constant depends on `w`"].
++Constant (fixed at the statement phase): `topDepth w = w·⌈log₂ w⌉ + ⌈log₂ w⌉ + 3`; r3's draft
++`+ 4` is one more than needed.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `toFormula_eq` and `TreeCircuit.depth_balanced_le` twice.  The inner `⋀_i` over
++`w` wires of depth `≤ T = (⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)·⌈log₂ l⌉ + 1` (`depth_compTree_le`) has depth
++`≤ T + ⌈log₂ w⌉ + 1`; the outer `⋁_f` over `|F| ≤ w^w` terms (`Finset.length_toList`,
++`Finset.card_le_univ`, `Fintype.card_fun`) adds `⌈log₂ |F|⌉ + 1 ≤ w·⌈log₂ w⌉ + 1`
++(`Nat.clog_mono_right`, `Nat.clog_le_of_le_pow` with `w^w ≤ (2^⌈log₂ w⌉)^w`, `Nat.le_pow_clog`);
++`topDepth_eq` closes the arithmetic. -/
++theorem depth_toFormula_le (B : BranchingProgram w n) (F : Finset (Fin w → Fin w)) :
++    (B.toFormula F).depth ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * Nat.clog 2 B.length + topDepth w := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C21.** Barrington's Theorem 2 formula has fan-in at most 2: `(B.toFormula F).maxFanin ≤ 2`
++[Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003: "a fan-in 2 circuit"].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `toFormula_eq`, `TreeCircuit.maxFanin_balanced_le` twice, and
++`maxFanin_compTree_le` for the leaves (`maxFaninL` of a mapped list). -/
++theorem maxFanin_toFormula_le (B : BranchingProgram w n) (F : Finset (Fin w → Fin w)) :
++    (B.toFormula F).maxFanin ≤ 2 := by
++  sorry
++
++end BranchingProgram

++/-
