++import Mathlib.Computability.Language
++import Mathlib.Data.Finset.Basic
++import Mathlib.Logic.Equiv.Defs
++
++/-!
++# Bounded-width branching programs
++
++The general branching-program model of [Bar89, §1]: an instruction reads one input bit and
++produces a map `[w] → [w]`; a width-`w` program is a finite sequence of instructions, and it
++*yields* on an input the composition of the maps its instructions produce.  A family of programs
++recognizes a language through accepting sets `F_n` of maps.  Permutation programs (every map a
++bijection) are the predicate `IsPermutation`.
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BranchingProgram.Instr` — an instruction `⟨var, map0, map1⟩` (BV14's order: `map0` when the
++  bit is `0`), with `Instr.eval`, `Instr.IsPermutation` and `Instr.ofPerm`.
++* `BranchingProgram` — a width-`w` program on `n` inputs (registered model); `length`, `append`
++  (the `++` notation), `yield`, `IsPermutation`, `conj` (Lemma 1's conjugation) and
++  `complementLast` (Lemma 2's change of the last instruction).
++* `BranchingProgramFamily` — a family with accepting sets (registered model); `language`,
++  `IsPolyLength`, `IsPermutation`.
++* `Language.InBWBP`, `Language.InPBP` — the classes BWBP and `PBP_w` (polynomial length).
++
++## Main results
++
++All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
++
++* `BranchingProgram.yield_append`, `yield_nil` — the yield composes in program order (C1).
++* `BranchingProgram.yield_conj`, `length_conj`, `length_append` — conjugation (C2).
++* `BranchingProgram.yield_complementLast`, `length_complementLast` — Lemma 2's step (C3).
++* `BranchingProgram.isPermutation_iff` (proved) and the closure lemmas of permutation
++  programs, `IsPermutation.bijective_yield` (C4).
++
++## Conventions and deviations
++
++* **Instruction order.** [Bar89, §1] writes `⟨j, f, g⟩` with `f` used when `x_j = 1`; the fields
++  here follow [BV14, §3.3]'s `(var(t), σ_{t,0}, σ_{t,1})`: `map0` is used when the bit is `0`.
++* **Program order.** [Bar89] writes products left to right (the leftmost map acts first).  The
++  model composes in program order: `(B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x`.  In Mathlib's
++  `Equiv.Perm` product (right factor first) Bar89's word `σ₁σ₂σ₁⁻¹σ₂⁻¹` is
++  `σ₂⁻¹ * σ₁⁻¹ * σ₂ * σ₁`.
++* **Recognition** is by accepting sets, as in [Bar89, §1]: `x ∈ L ↔ B(x) ∈ F_n`.
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
++  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false
++
++/-- An instruction `⟨var, map0, map1⟩` of width `w` over `n` inputs: read input `var`; apply
++`map0 : [w] → [w]` if it is `0` and `map1` if it is `1` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
++Deviation: Bar89 lists the `x_j = 1` map first; the fields follow BV14's
++`(var(t), σ_{t,0}, σ_{t,1})` [BV14, §3.3, p013-b002]. -/
++structure BranchingProgram.Instr (w n : ℕ) where
++  /-- The input bit the instruction reads. -/
++  var : Fin n
++  /-- The map produced when the bit is `0`. -/
++  map0 : Fin w → Fin w
++  /-- The map produced when the bit is `1`. -/
++  map1 : Fin w → Fin w
++
++/-- A width-`w` branching program on `n` inputs: a finite sequence of instructions
++`instrs = [ι₁, …, ι_l]` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002].  Registered model
++(`TCSlib/ComputationalModels.lean`). -/
++structure BranchingProgram (w n : ℕ) where
++  /-- The instructions, in program order. -/
++  instrs : List (BranchingProgram.Instr w n)
++
++namespace BranchingProgram
++
++variable {w n : ℕ}
++
++/-! ### Instructions -/
++
++/-- The map an instruction produces on input `x`: `ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0`
++[Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "evaluate to `f` if `x_j = 1`, otherwise evaluate to `g`"; BV14 order]. -/
++def Instr.eval (ι : Instr w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) : Fin w → Fin w :=
++  if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0
++
++/-- Reader of `Instr.eval`: `ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0`. -/
++theorem Instr.eval_eq (ι : Instr w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0 := rfl
++
++/-- `ι` is a permutation instruction: both of its maps are bijections,
++`Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
++def Instr.IsPermutation (ι : Instr w n) : Prop :=
++  Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1
++
++/-- Reader of `Instr.IsPermutation`:
++`ι.IsPermutation ↔ Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`. -/
++theorem Instr.isPermutation_iff (ι : Instr w n) :
++    ι.IsPermutation ↔ Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1 := Iff.rfl
++
++/-- The instruction `⟨i, σ₀, σ₁⟩` with permutation maps: `Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁` reads input `i`
++and produces `σ₀` on `0` and `σ₁` on `1` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001; BV14, §3.3, p013-b002].  Its
++field readers are `Instr.ofPerm_var`, `Instr.ofPerm_map0`, `Instr.ofPerm_map1`. -/
++@[simps (config := { isSimp := false })]
++def Instr.ofPerm (i : Fin n) (σ₀ σ₁ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : Instr w n where
++  var := i
++  map0 := σ₀
++  map1 := σ₁
++
++/-- An instruction built from permutations is a permutation instruction:
++`(Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁).IsPermutation`.  Glue. -/
++theorem Instr.isPermutation_ofPerm (i : Fin n) (σ₀ σ₁ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    (Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁ : Instr w n).IsPermutation :=
++  ⟨σ₀.bijective, σ₁.bijective⟩
++
++/-! ### Programs -/
++
++/-- The length of a program, its number of instructions: `B.length = B.instrs.length`
++[Bar89, §1, p001-b002]. -/
++def length (B : BranchingProgram w n) : ℕ :=
++  B.instrs.length
++
++/-- Reader of `length`: `B.length = B.instrs.length`. -/
++theorem length_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) : B.length = B.instrs.length := rfl
++
++/-- Concatenation of programs, `B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩`: run `B₁`, then `B₂`
++(Bar89's juxtaposition `B₁B₂`, [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002]).  It is the `++` notation. -/
++def append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) : BranchingProgram w n :=
++  ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩
++
++instance : Append (BranchingProgram w n) := ⟨append⟩
++
++/-- Reader of `append`: `B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩`. -/
++theorem append_eq (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
++    B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩ := rfl
++
++/-- Reader of the `++` notation: `(B₁ ++ B₂).instrs = B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs`. -/
++theorem append_instrs (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
++    (B₁ ++ B₂).instrs = B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs := rfl
++
++/-- The map `B` yields on `x`: the composition, in program order, of the maps its instructions
++produce, `B.yield x = ι_l.eval x ∘ ⋯ ∘ ι₁.eval x`, computed as the left fold
++`B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id`; `yield ⟨[]⟩ x = id` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002:
++"the composition of the functions produced by each of the instructions"].
++Deviation: Bar89's products read left to right; here the first instruction acts first. -/
++def yield (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) : Fin w → Fin w :=
++  B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id
++
++/-- Reader of `yield`: `B.yield x = B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id`. -/
++theorem yield_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    B.yield x = B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id := rfl
++
++/-- `B` is a permutation branching program: every map of every instruction is a bijection,
++`∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`
++[Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
++def IsPermutation (B : BranchingProgram w n) : Prop :=
++  ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1
++
++/-- Conjugation of a program by `θ`: every map `f` of every instruction becomes `θ ∘ f ∘ θ⁻¹`,
++`B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩`
++[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003: "replacing each `σ_i` and `τ_i` by `θσ_iθ⁻¹` and
++`θτ_iθ⁻¹`"]. -/
++def conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : BranchingProgram w n :=
++  ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩
++
++/-- Reader of `conj`:
++`B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩`. -/
++theorem conj_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩ :=
++  rfl
++
++/-- Lemma 2's change of the last instruction: the last instruction `⟨i, μ, ν⟩` becomes
++`⟨i, σ⁻¹ ∘ μ, σ⁻¹ ∘ ν⟩`, every other instruction is kept, and the empty program is unchanged:
++`B.complementLast σ = ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩` when
++`ι` is the last instruction [Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004: "`⟨i, μσ⁻¹, νσ⁻¹⟩`"].
++Deviation: Bar89's `μσ⁻¹` is read left to right (first `μ`, then `σ⁻¹`), so it is the map
++`σ⁻¹ ∘ μ` here; the definition is total (Bar89 assumes a last instruction). -/
++def complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    BranchingProgram w n :=
++  match B.instrs.getLast? with
++  | none => B
++  | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩
++
++/-- Reader of `complementLast`, by cases on the last instruction:
++`B.complementLast σ = match B.instrs.getLast? with | none => B | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++
++[⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩`. -/
++theorem complementLast_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    B.complementLast σ = match B.instrs.getLast? with
++      | none => B
++      | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩ := rfl
++
++/-- `complementLast` on the empty program: `(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
++⟨[]⟩`. -/
++theorem complementLast_nil (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ = ⟨[]⟩ := rfl
++
++/-- `complementLast` on a program with last instruction `ι`:
++`(⟨l ++ [ι]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
++⟨l ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩`.  With `complementLast_nil` it covers every
++program (`List.eq_nil_or_concat`). -/
++theorem complementLast_concat (l : List (Instr w n)) (ι : Instr w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    (⟨l ++ [ι]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
++      ⟨l ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩ := by
++  rw [complementLast_eq]
++  simp only [List.getLast?_concat, List.dropLast_concat]
++
++/-! ### Targets C1–C4 -/
++
++/-- **C1.** The yield of a concatenation composes in program order:
++`(B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "the composition of the
++functions produced by each of the instructions"].
++Deviation: Bar89 writes products left to right; in Mathlib's `Equiv.Perm` product (right factor
++first) the yield of `B₁B₂` on permutations is `B₂(x) * B₁(x)` (r3 §H3.6).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `append_instrs` and `yield_eq`; `List.foldl_append` turns the left
++side into a fold over `B₂.instrs` started at `B₁.yield x`.  An auxiliary induction on a list `l`
++shows `l.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) g = l.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id ∘ g`
++(composition is associative, `Function.comp_assoc`), which with `g = B₁.yield x` closes the goal. -/
++theorem yield_append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C1.** The empty program yields the identity: `(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).yield x = id`
++[Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `yield_eq` and `List.foldl_nil`. -/
++theorem yield_nil (x : Fin n → Bool) : (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).yield x = id := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C2.** Conjugating every map by `θ` conjugates the yield:
++`(B.conj θ).yield x = θ ∘ B.yield x ∘ θ.symm` [Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `conj_eq` and `yield_eq` and induct on `B.instrs` with the fold
++generalized to an arbitrary start map `g` (the claim becomes `foldl … (θ ∘ g ∘ θ.symm) =
++θ ∘ foldl … g ∘ θ.symm`).  The step uses `Instr.eval_eq`, splits on `x ι.var`, and cancels the
++inner `θ.symm ∘ θ` by `Equiv.symm_comp_self`. -/
++theorem yield_conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (B.conj θ).yield x = θ ∘ B.yield x ∘ θ.symm := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C2.** Conjugation keeps the length: `(B.conj θ).length = B.length`
++[Bar89, Lemma 1, p003-b003: "of the same length as `B`"].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `length_eq`, `conj_eq` and `List.length_map`. -/
++theorem length_conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    (B.conj θ).length = B.length := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C2.** Lengths add under concatenation: `(B₁ ++ B₂).length = B₁.length + B₂.length`
++[Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `length_eq`, `append_instrs` and `List.length_append`. -/
++theorem length_append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
++    (B₁ ++ B₂).length = B₁.length + B₂.length := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C3.** Lemma 2's step post-composes the yield with `σ⁻¹`: for a nonempty program,
++`(B.complementLast σ).yield x = σ.symm ∘ B.yield x` [Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004:
++"`B'(x) = e` if `x ∈ A` and `B'(x) = σ⁻¹` if `x ∉ A`"].
++Deviation: Bar89's `B(x)σ⁻¹` is read left to right; in program order it is `σ⁻¹ ∘ B(x)`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Write `B.instrs = l ++ [ι]` (`List.eq_nil_or_concat`; the empty case contradicts
++`hB` through `length_eq`).  Then `complementLast_concat`, `yield_eq` and `List.foldl_append`
++reduce both sides to the last step: the new last map is `σ.symm ∘ ι.eval x` by `Instr.eval_eq`
++(split on `x ι.var`), and composition is associative. -/
++theorem yield_complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w))
++    (hB : 0 < B.length) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (B.complementLast σ).yield x = σ.symm ∘ B.yield x := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C3.** Lemma 2's step keeps the length: `(B.complementLast σ).length = B.length`
++[Bar89, Lemma 2, p003-b004: "in length `l`"].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Cases by `List.eq_nil_or_concat` on `B.instrs`: `complementLast_nil` is the
++identity, and `complementLast_concat` replaces one instruction (`List.length_append`). -/
++theorem length_complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
++    (B.complementLast σ).length = B.length := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C4.** Reader of `IsPermutation` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]:
++`B.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`.
++It is the definition's reader and is proved (`Iff.rfl`). -/
++theorem isPermutation_iff (B : BranchingProgram w n) :
++    B.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1 :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++/-- **C4.** Permutation programs are closed under concatenation: if `B₁` and `B₂` are permutation
++programs, so is `B₁ ++ B₂`.  Glue for [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `isPermutation_iff`, `append_instrs` and `List.mem_append`. -/
++theorem IsPermutation.append {B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n} (h₁ : B₁.IsPermutation)
++    (h₂ : B₂.IsPermutation) : (B₁ ++ B₂).IsPermutation := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C4.** Conjugation keeps a permutation program a permutation program:
++`B.IsPermutation → (B.conj θ).IsPermutation`.  Glue for [Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `isPermutation_iff`, `conj_eq`, `List.mem_map`; each new map `θ ∘ f ∘ θ.symm` is
++a composition of bijections (`Function.Bijective.comp`, `Equiv.bijective`). -/
++theorem IsPermutation.conj {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
++    (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : (B.conj θ).IsPermutation := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C4.** Lemma 2's step keeps a permutation program a permutation program:
++`B.IsPermutation → (B.complementLast σ).IsPermutation`.  Glue for
++[Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Cases by `List.eq_nil_or_concat`; with `complementLast_concat` the instructions of
++`l` are unchanged and the new last maps `σ.symm ∘ f` are compositions of bijections
++(`Function.Bijective.comp`). -/
++theorem IsPermutation.complementLast {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
++    (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : (B.complementLast σ).IsPermutation := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C4.** A permutation program yields a bijection on every input:
++`B.IsPermutation → Function.Bijective (B.yield x)`.  Glue for [Bar89, §1, p002-b001].
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `B.instrs` with the fold generalized to a bijective start map:
++`yield_eq`, and at each step `Instr.eval_eq` picks `map0` or `map1`, bijective by
++`isPermutation_iff`, so the composite stays bijective (`Function.Bijective.comp`). -/
++theorem IsPermutation.bijective_yield {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
++    (x : Fin n → Bool) : Function.Bijective (B.yield x) := by
++  sorry
++
++end BranchingProgram
++
++/-! ### Families and classes -/
++
++/-- A family of width-`w` branching programs with accepting sets: `program n` takes `n` inputs and
++`accept n ⊆ ([w] → [w])` is its accepting set; the family recognizes
++`{u | (program |u|).yield u.get ∈ accept |u|}` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "`x ∈ L` iff
++`B(x) ∈ F_n`"].  Registered model (`TCSlib/ComputationalModels.lean`). -/
++structure BranchingProgramFamily (w : ℕ) where
++  /-- The program for inputs of length `n`. -/
++  program : (n : ℕ) → BranchingProgram w n
++  /-- The accepting set `F_n` for inputs of length `n`. -/
++  accept : (n : ℕ) → Finset (Fin w → Fin w)
++
++namespace BranchingProgramFamily
++
++variable {w : ℕ} (P : BranchingProgramFamily w)
++
++/-- The language a family recognizes:
++`P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}`
++[Bar89, §1, p001-b002]. -/
++def language : Language Bool :=
++  {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}
++
++/-- Reader of `language`:
++`u ∈ P.language ↔ (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length`. -/
++theorem mem_language_iff (u : List Bool) :
++    u ∈ P.language ↔ (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++/-- The language unfolded:
++`P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}`. -/
++theorem language_eq :
++    P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length} := rfl
++
++/-- The family has polynomial length: `∃ a k, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k`
++[Bar89, §1, p002-b001] (the `(n + 1)` form of `PPoly.lean`'s polynomial size). -/
++def IsPolyLength : Prop :=
++  ∃ a k : ℕ, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k
++
++/-- Reader of `IsPolyLength`:
++`P.IsPolyLength ↔ ∃ a k, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k`. -/
++theorem isPolyLength_iff :
++    P.IsPolyLength ↔ ∃ a k : ℕ, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++/-- Every program of the family is a permutation program: `∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation`
++[Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
++def IsPermutation : Prop :=
++  ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation
++
++/-- Reader of `IsPermutation`: `P.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation`. -/
++theorem isPermutation_iff : P.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++end BranchingProgramFamily
++
++/-- `L ∈ BWBP`: for some width `w`, a family of width-`w` programs of polynomial length
++(`length ≤ a·(n+1)^k`) recognizes `L`,
++`∃ w (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
++def Language.InBWBP (L : Language Bool) : Prop :=
++  ∃ (w : ℕ) (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L
++
++/-- Reader of `Language.InBWBP`:
++`L.InBWBP ↔ ∃ w (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L`. -/
++theorem Language.inBWBP_iff (L : Language Bool) :
++    L.InBWBP ↔ ∃ (w : ℕ) (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L :=
++  Iff.rfl
++
++/-- `L ∈ PBP_w`: a family of width-`w` permutation programs of polynomial length
++(`length ≤ a·(n+1)^k`) recognizes `L`,
++`∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L`
++[Bar89, §1, p002-b001; Corollary, p004-b004: "polynomial-length 5-PBPs"]. -/
++def Language.InPBP (w : ℕ) (L : Language Bool) : Prop :=
++  ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L
++
++/-- Reader of `Language.InPBP`:
++`L.InPBP w ↔ ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧
++P.language = L`. -/
++theorem Language.inPBP_iff (w : ℕ) (L : Language Bool) :
++    L.InPBP w ↔
++      ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L :=
++  Iff.rfl

++import Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Group.Finset.Basic
++import Mathlib.Logic.Equiv.Basic
++import TCSlib.Complexity.BranchingPrograms.Defs
++
++/-!
++# The branching-program host `evalWith`
++
++A program run on a *state vector* `V : Fin w → α` instead of a single state: one instruction
++sends `V` to the vector whose coordinate `i` collects, through a selector `sel` applied to the
++instruction's input, the sums of the entries that the `0`-branch and the `1`-branch send to `i`
++(the push form).  Folding this step over the instructions in program order is the host
++`BranchingProgram.evalWith`, generic in the selector `sel : β → α → α → α` and in the value type
++`α` (an additive commutative monoid).  It is row H18 of the host registry
++(`machine-library-design.md` §14).  Its consumers are the Boolean semantics (C8: a one-hot state
++stays one-hot at the yield, for every program) and GSW's homomorphic evaluation of a permutation
++program, where `α` is a ciphertext type and `sel` the homomorphic selector.
++
++The push form needs no inverse, so the host is total and computable on the general model of
++[Bar89, §1]; for a permutation instruction each sum has exactly one term and the step is
++[BV14, Eq. (4)]'s pull form (C6).
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BranchingProgram.Instr.stepWith` — one instruction on a state vector (push form).
++* `BranchingProgram.evalWith` — the host: `stepWith` folded in program order.
++
++## Main results
++
++All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
++
++* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_nil`, `evalWith_append` — readers of the fold (C5).
++* `BranchingProgram.Instr.stepWith_of_isPermutation` — BV14's pull form for a permutation
++  instruction (C6).
++* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_rel` — the step-indexed relational host lemma (C7).
++* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_single` — Boolean semantics for general maps (C8).
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
++  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.

++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false
++
++namespace BranchingProgram
++
++open Finset
++
++variable {w n : ℕ} {α α' β β' : Type*}
++
++/-- One instruction on a state vector, push form: coordinate `i` of the new vector is
++`sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), V j) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map1 · = i), V j)`,
++the selector applied to the instruction's input and to the sums of the entries that the
++`0`-branch and the `1`-branch send to `i`.  Host step; glue generalizing
++[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002] from permutations to general maps. -/
++def Instr.stepWith [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β) (ι : Instr w n)
++    (V : Fin w → α) : Fin w → α :=
++  fun i => sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map0 j = i), V j)
++    (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map1 j = i), V j)
++
++/-- Reader of `Instr.stepWith`: `ι.stepWith sel inp V i =
++sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), V j) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map1 · = i), V j)`.
++-/
++theorem Instr.stepWith_apply [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
++    (ι : Instr w n) (V : Fin w → α) (i : Fin w) :
++    ι.stepWith sel inp V i = sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map0 j = i), V j)
++      (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map1 j = i), V j) := rfl
++
++/-- **The branching-program host** (registry row H18): fold `Instr.stepWith sel inp` over the
++instructions in program order, starting from `V`,
++`B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V`.  Generic in the
++selector `sel` and the value type `α`; its consumers are the Boolean semantics (C8) and GSW's
++homomorphic evaluation (G2).  Glue generalizing [BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002]. -/
++def evalWith [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
++    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) : Fin w → α :=
++  B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V
++
++/-- Reader of `evalWith`: `B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U)
++V`. -/
++theorem evalWith_eq [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
++    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) :
++    B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V := rfl
++
++/-! ### Targets C5–C8 -/
++
++/-- **C5.** The empty program leaves the state unchanged:
++`(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).evalWith sel inp V = V`.  Glue (reader of the fold).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `evalWith_eq` and `List.foldl_nil`. -/
++theorem evalWith_nil [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
++    (V : Fin w → α) : (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).evalWith sel inp V = V := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C5.** The host runs a concatenation in program order:
++`(B₁ ++ B₂).evalWith sel inp V = B₂.evalWith sel inp (B₁.evalWith sel inp V)`.  Glue (reader of
++the fold).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `evalWith_eq`, the reader `append_instrs` of `++`, and `List.foldl_append`. -/
++theorem evalWith_append [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
++    (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) :
++    (B₁ ++ B₂).evalWith sel inp V = B₂.evalWith sel inp (B₁.evalWith sel inp V) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C6.** For a permutation instruction each sum of the push form has exactly one term, and the
++step is BV14's pull form: `ι.stepWith sel inp V i = sel (inp ι.var) (V (σ₀⁻¹ i)) (V (σ₁⁻¹ i))`
++with `σ₀ = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map0 _`, `σ₁ = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map1 _`
++[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002: `v_t[i] := v_{t−1}[σ_{t,0}⁻¹(i)]·(1 − x) + v_{t−1}[σ_{t,1}⁻¹(i)]·x`].
++Deviation: BV14's selector is the arithmetic `(1 − x)·u + x·v`; here it is the abstract `sel`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `Instr.stepWith_apply`.  With `e = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map0 _`
++(bijective by `Instr.isPermutation_iff`), `ι.map0 j = i ↔ j = e.symm i`
++(`Equiv.apply_eq_iff_eq_symm_apply`), so the filter is the singleton `{e.symm i}`
++(`Finset.filter_eq'`) and the sum is `V (e.symm i)` (`Finset.sum_singleton`); the same for
++`map1`. -/
++theorem Instr.stepWith_of_isPermutation [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α)
++    (inp : Fin n → β) {ι : Instr w n} (hι : ι.IsPermutation) (V : Fin w → α) (i : Fin w) :
++    ι.stepWith sel inp V i =
++      sel (inp ι.var) (V ((Equiv.ofBijective _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).1).symm i))
++        (V ((Equiv.ofBijective _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).2).symm i)) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C7.** **Step-indexed relational host lemma**: two runs of the host on the same program,
++possibly with different selectors, inputs and value types, that are related by `R 0` at the
++start and whose relation is carried from `R t` to `R (t + 1)` by every instruction
++`B.instrs[t]`, end related by `R B.length`:
++`R 0 V V' → (∀ t < B.length, ∀ U U', R t U U' → R (t + 1) (ι_t.stepWith sel inp U)
++(ι_t.stepWith sel' inp' U')) → R B.length (B.evalWith sel inp V) (B.evalWith sel' inp' V')`.
++Glue; GSW carries its noise bound `t·2Nβ` in the index.
++
++**Proof sketch.** By `evalWith_eq` both sides are left folds over `B.instrs`.  Prove, by
++induction on `k ≤ B.instrs.length`, that the folds over `B.instrs.take k` are related by `R k`:
++the step `k → k + 1` uses `List.take_succ`, `List.foldl_append` and `hstep k` with
++`B.instrs[k]`; at `k = B.instrs.length` use `List.take_length` and `length_eq`. -/
++theorem evalWith_rel [AddCommMonoid α] [AddCommMonoid α'] (sel : β → α → α → α)
++    (sel' : β' → α' → α' → α') (inp : Fin n → β) (inp' : Fin n → β')
++    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (R : ℕ → (Fin w → α) → (Fin w → α') → Prop) (V : Fin w → α)
++    (V' : Fin w → α') (h0 : R 0 V V')
++    (hstep : ∀ t (ht : t < B.length) (U : Fin w → α) (U' : Fin w → α'), R t U U' →
++      R (t + 1) ((B.instrs[t]'(ht.trans_eq B.length_eq)).stepWith sel inp U)
++        ((B.instrs[t]'(ht.trans_eq B.length_eq)).stepWith sel' inp' U')) :
++    R B.length (B.evalWith sel inp V) (B.evalWith sel' inp' V') := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C8.** Boolean semantics for **general** maps: with values in `ℕ` and the selector
++`sel b u v = if b then v else u`, the host started at the one-hot vector of `s` ends at the
++one-hot vector of `B.yield x s`:
++`B.evalWith (fun b u v => if b then v else u) x (Pi.single s 1) = Pi.single (B.yield x s) 1`
++[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002, generalized from permutations; Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
++Deviation: BV14 states Eq. (4) for permutations; the push form makes it hold for every map.
++
++**Proof sketch.** One step: for every instruction `ι`,
++`ι.stepWith sel x (Pi.single s 1) = Pi.single (ι.eval x s) 1`; at coordinate `i` the `0`-sum is
++`∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), Pi.single s 1 j = if ι.map0 s = i then 1 else 0`
++(`Finset.sum_filter`, `Pi.single_apply`, `Finset.sum_ite_eq'`), likewise the `1`-sum, and the
++selector picks the branch of `x ι.var` as `Instr.eval_eq` does.  Then induct on `B.instrs` with
++the yield's fold generalized to a start map `f` (claim: the host fold from `Pi.single (f s) 1`
++is `Pi.single (fold f s) 1`), using `evalWith_eq` and `yield_eq`. -/
++theorem evalWith_single (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) (s : Fin w) :
++    B.evalWith (fun b (u v : ℕ) => if b then v else u) x (Pi.single s 1) =
++      Pi.single (B.yield x s) 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++end BranchingProgram

++import TCSlib.Complexity.BranchingPrograms.Barrington

++/-!
++# Bounded-width branching programs and NC¹
++
++The class-level consequences of Barrington's two theorems [Bar89, §3]: the `DAGCircuit` form of
++Theorem 1 (length `≤ 4^depth`), NC¹ ⊆ polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs, BWBP ⊆ NC¹
++(Theorem 2 for families), and Barrington's Corollary BWBP = nonuniform NC¹ = PBP₅.  The classes
++`Language.InBWBP`, `Language.InPBP` are defined in `Defs.lean`; `Language.InNC` is the book's
++`DAGCircuit` class (`NCAC.lean`).  Both directions go through the formula class
++`Language.InTreeNC 1` (`Language.inNC_one_iff`).
++
++## Main definitions
++
++None: the classes are defined in `Defs.lean`, the constructions in `Barrington.lean` and
++`BarringtonConverse.lean`.
++
++## Main results
++
++All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`).
++
++* `BranchingProgram.exists_fiveCycleRecognizes_of_dagCircuit` — Thm 1 for `DAGCircuit`s (C22).
++* `Language.InNC.inPBP_five` — NC¹ ⊆ PBP₅ (C23).
++* `Language.InBWBP.inNC_one` — BWBP ⊆ NC¹ (C24).
++* `Language.inNC_one_iff_inBWBP`, `Language.inNC_one_iff_inPBP_five` — the Corollary (C25).
++
++## Deviations
++
++* Inputs of length `0` have no variable for Barrington's constants to read (`[NeZero n]`); the
++  family statements handle `n = 0` through the accepting set (the empty program yields `id`, and
++  `F_0` is `{id}` or `∅` according to whether `[] ∈ L`).
++* The circuit of Theorem 2 is a formula (P6-Q18); it reaches `Language.InNC 1` through
++  `Language.InTreeNC.inNC`.
++
++## References
++
++* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
++  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
++* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
++  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
++-/
++
++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false

++namespace BranchingProgram

++open BoolCircuit Equiv

++/-- **C22.** **The `DAGCircuit` corollary of Theorem 1**: a fan-in-two circuit `C` on `n ≥ 1`
++inputs is five-cycle recognized, with any five-cycle output `σ`, by a width-5 permutation program
++of length at most `4 ^ C.depth`:
++`∃ B, B.IsPermutation ∧ B.length ≤ 4 ^ C.depth ∧ FiveCycleRecognizes B (fun x => C.eval x = true) σ`
++[BV14, Thm 3.5, p014-b001; Bar89, Thm 1, p003-b002].
++Deviation: Bar89 and BV14 start from a circuit of AND/OR (BV14: NAND) gates; here the circuit is
++unfolded to a formula first (`DAGCircuit.toTree`, depth-preserving), P6-Q2.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Take `B = barrington C.toTree σ`.  From `hC` (`DAGCircuit.isFaninTwo_iff`, and
++the reader `DAGCircuit.isWellFormed_iff`, missing at the base and proposed) get
++well-formedness and fan-in `≤ 2` of every gate; then `toTree_maxFanin_le` gives
++`C.toTree.maxFanin ≤ 2`, `toTree_depth_le` gives `C.toTree.depth ≤ C.depth`, and `toTree_eval`
++gives `C.toTree.eval x = C.eval x`.  Conclude by `isPermutation_barrington`,
++`barrington_length_le` with `Nat.pow_le_pow_right`, and `barrington_recognizes`. -/
++theorem exists_fiveCycleRecognizes_of_dagCircuit {n : ℕ} [NeZero n] (C : DAGCircuit n)
++    (hC : C.IsFaninTwo) {σ : Perm (Fin 5)} (hσ : IsFiveCycle σ) :
++    ∃ B : BranchingProgram 5 n, B.IsPermutation ∧ B.length ≤ 4 ^ C.depth ∧
++      FiveCycleRecognizes B (fun x => C.eval x = true) σ := by
++  sorry
++
++end BranchingProgram
++
++open BoolCircuit BranchingProgram
++
++/-- **C23.** **NC¹ ⊆ polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs**: `L.InNC 1 → L.InPBP 5`
++[Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004; abstract, p001-b001].
++Deviation: inputs of length `0` are handled by the accepting set (Thm 1 needs `n ≥ 1`).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `Language.InNC.inTreeNC_one` gives a fan-in-2 formula family `C` with depth
++`≤ b·(log₂ n + 1)`, deciding `L` (read through readers of `Language.InTreeNC`,
++`TreeCircuitFamily.HasFaninTwo` and `HasPolylogDepth`, missing at the base and proposed).
++Define the family with `program n = barrington (C.circuit n) sigma1` for `n ≥ 1` (`NeZero`
++from `n ≠ 0`) and `⟨[]⟩` for `n = 0`; `accept n = {sigma1}` for `n ≥ 1`, and `{id}` or `∅`
++at `n = 0` according to `[] ∈ L` (classical).  Permutation: `isPermutation_barrington`
++(`BranchingProgramFamily.isPermutation_iff`).
++Length: `barrington_length_le` and `4 ^ (b·(log₂ n + 1)) ≤ 4 ^ b · (n + 1) ^ (2b)`
++(`Nat.pow_le_pow_right`, `Nat.pow_log_le_self`), so `BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff`.
++Language: `BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff`, `barrington_recognizes` with
++`isFiveCycle_sigma1`, `TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff`; at `n = 0`, `yield_nil`.
++Conclude with `Language.inPBP_iff`. -/
++theorem Language.InNC.inPBP_five {L : Language Bool} (h : L.InNC 1) : L.InPBP 5 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C24.** **BWBP ⊆ NC¹**, Theorem 2 for families: `L.InBWBP → L.InNC 1`
++[Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003; abstract, p001-b001: "any bounded-width polynomial-size branching
++program can be simulated by an `NC¹` circuit"].
++Deviation: the circuits are fan-in-2 formulas (P6-Q18), compiled to `DAGCircuit`s by
++`Language.InTreeNC.inNC`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** From `Language.inBWBP_iff` get `w`, `P` and a length bound `a·(n+1)^k`
++(`BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff`).  The formula family `n ↦ (P.program n).toFormula
++(P.accept n)` decides `P.language` (`toFormula_eval`, `BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff`,
++`TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff`), has fan-in 2 (`maxFanin_toFormula_le`), and depth
++`≤ (⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)·⌈log₂ (a(n+1)^k)⌉ + topDepth w ≤ b·(log₂ n + 1)` for a constant `b`
++(`depth_toFormula_le`, `Nat.clog_mono_right`, `BoolCircuit.clog_poly_le`); its size is
++`≤ 2 ^ (depth + 1)` (`TreeCircuit.size_succ_le_two_pow`), hence polynomial.  This gives
++`L.InTreeNC 1` (through the proposed readers of `Language.InTreeNC` and the `TreeCircuitFamily`
++predicates, missing at the base), and `Language.InTreeNC.inNC` gives `L.InNC 1`. -/
++theorem Language.InBWBP.inNC_one {L : Language Bool} (h : L.InBWBP) : L.InNC 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C25.** **Barrington's Corollary**, first equality: nonuniform NC¹ is BWBP,
++`L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InBWBP` [Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004].
++
++**Proof sketch.** `→`: `Language.InNC.inPBP_five`, then forget permutation-ness and take `w = 5`
++(`Language.inPBP_iff`, `Language.inBWBP_iff`).  `←`: `Language.InBWBP.inNC_one`. -/
++theorem Language.inNC_one_iff_inBWBP (L : Language Bool) : L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InBWBP := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **C25.** **Barrington's Corollary**, second equality: nonuniform NC¹ is the class of
++polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs, `L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InPBP 5`
++[Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004].

++theorem Language.inNC_one_iff_inPBP_five (L : Language Bool) : L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InPBP 5 := by

++import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.Adder

++/-!
++# Formula arithmetic: carry-save addition
++
++Numbers as little-endian lists of `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit` formulas, and the carry-save (3:2)
++reduction of many such numbers to two.  A list `U` denotes, under the assignment `x`, the number
++`FormulaArith.val x U = bitsVal (U.map (·.eval x))` (`Adder.lean`'s `bitsVal`).  One carry-save
++step turns three numbers `A, B, C` into two, the bitwise sum `S` (`xor3`) and the shifted
++carries `K` (`maj3`), with `val A + val B + val C = val S + val K` at a cost of four levels of
++depth and none in the bit length.  Applying a layer of steps to the triples of a list of `k`
++numbers, `csaLevels k ≤ 2⌈log₂ k⌉` times, leaves two numbers with the same total.  This is the
++summation step of the decryption formula ([BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof]; r3 §H3.3–H3.4): its depth is
++logarithmic in the number of summands and does not depend on their bit length.
++
++## Main definitions
++
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.val` — the number a list of formulas denotes.
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaStep` — one 3:2 step `(A, B, C) ↦ (S, K)`.
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaLayer` — one layer of steps over a list of numbers.
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaLevels` — the number of layers needed to reach two numbers.
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaReduce` — the reduction of a list of numbers to two.
++
++## Main results
++
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaStep_val`, `depth_csaStep_le`, `maxFaninL_csaStep_le` — F5:
++  the value equation, depth `+4`, fan-in two (targets).
++* `BoolCircuit.FormulaArith.csaReduce_val`, `depth_csaReduce_le`, `maxFaninL_csaReduce_le`,
++  `csaLevels_le` — F6: the total is kept, depth `4 · csaLevels k` over the inputs (at least
++  `1`), and `csaLevels k ≤ 2⌈log₂ k⌉` (targets).
++
++## References
++
++* [BV11] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Efficient Fully Homomorphic Encryption from
++  (Standard) LWE*, FOCS 2011, pp. 97–106; full version, IACR ePrint 2011/344 (39 pages,
++  2 March 2012), §4.5, Lemma 4.5 and its proof.
++-/

++set_option maxHeartbeats 0
++set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
++set_option autoImplicit false

++namespace BoolCircuit

++namespace FormulaArith

++variable {n : ℕ}

++/-! ### Numbers denoted by lists of formulas -/
++
++/-- The number denoted by a little-endian list of formulas under the assignment `x`: formula
++`j` is bit `j`, of weight `2 ^ j`, `val x U = bitsVal (U.map (·.eval x))` (`Adder.lean`).
++Glue. -/
++def val (x : Fin n → Bool) (U : List (TreeCircuit n)) : ℕ :=
++  bitsVal (U.map fun c => c.eval x)
++
++/-- The value of a list of formulas is `bitsVal` of their values:
++`val x U = bitsVal (U.map fun c => c.eval x)`. -/
++theorem val_eq (x : Fin n → Bool) (U : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    val x U = bitsVal (U.map fun c => c.eval x) := rfl
++
++/-! ### One carry-save step (F5) -/
++
++/-- One carry-save (3:2) step on three little-endian numbers: with `L` the longest length and
++every number padded by `const false` to `L` bits, the sum word is the bitwise `xor3`,
++`S_i = xor3 A_i B_i C_i` (`i < L`), and the carry word is the bitwise `maj3` shifted up by one,
++`K = const false :: [maj3 A_i B_i C_i | i < L]`; so `|S| = L` and `|K| = L + 1`.
++`csaStep A B C = (S, K)` with `val A + val B + val C = val S + val K` (`csaStep_val`).
++[BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof, p029-b002]; the carry-save step is the deviation of r3 §H3.4. -/
++def csaStep (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n)) : List (TreeCircuit n) × List (TreeCircuit n) :=
++  ((List.range (max A.length (max B.length C.length))).map fun i =>
++      TreeCircuit.xor3 (A.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)) (B.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false))
++        (C.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)),
++    TreeCircuit.const false ::
++      (List.range (max A.length (max B.length C.length))).map fun i =>
++        TreeCircuit.maj3 (A.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false))
++          (B.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)) (C.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)))
++
++/-- The carry-save step, written out: with `L = max |A| (max |B| |C|)` and `z = const false`,
++`csaStep A B C = ((range L).map fun i => xor3 (A.getD i z) (B.getD i z) (C.getD i z),
++z :: (range L).map fun i => maj3 (A.getD i z) (B.getD i z) (C.getD i z))`. -/
++theorem csaStep_eq (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    csaStep A B C =
++      ((List.range (max A.length (max B.length C.length))).map fun i =>
++          TreeCircuit.xor3 (A.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false))
++            (B.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)) (C.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)),
++        TreeCircuit.const false ::
++          (List.range (max A.length (max B.length C.length))).map fun i =>
++            TreeCircuit.maj3 (A.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false))
++              (B.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false)) (C.getD i (TreeCircuit.const false))) :=
++  rfl
++
++/-- **F5, value.** One carry-save step keeps the total: for every assignment `x`,
++`val x A + val x B + val x C = val x (csaStep A B C).1 + val x (csaStep A B C).2`.
++[BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof, p029-b002: "The summation (over the integers) can be done in depth
++O(log k + log log p)"].  *Deviation:* the paper does not say how; r3 §H3.4 fixes the carry-save
++(3:2) tree, of which this is one step.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Write every value through `val_eq` and `bitsVal_eq_prefixVal` as a
++`prefixVal` of its bit sequence, extended to `L + 1` bits by `false` (`prefixVal_congr`; the
++padding `const false` evaluates to `false` by `const_eval`).  Bit by bit, the full-adder
++identity `a + b + c = (a ⊕ b ⊕ c) + 2 · maj(a, b, c)` holds by cases on three Booleans, with
++`xor3_eval` and `maj3_eval` giving the values of `S_i` and `K_{i+1}`; summing it with weights
++`2 ^ i` (induction on the number of bits, `prefixVal_succ`) gives the equation. -/
++theorem csaStep_val (x : Fin n → Bool) (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    val x A + val x B + val x C = val x (csaStep A B C).1 + val x (csaStep A B C).2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F5, depth `+4`.** Both output words of a carry-save step lie at most four levels above
++the inputs (counted from at least `1`, the depth of the padding constants):
++`TreeCircuit.maxDepth ((csaStep A B C).1 ++ (csaStep A B C).2) ≤
++max 1 (TreeCircuit.maxDepth (A ++ B ++ C)) + 4`.  [BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof, p029-b002];
++r3's `+4` *(fix)*, settled: `xor3` is `+4`, `maj3` is `+3`, the constant `const false` has
++depth `1`.  No term depends on the bit length.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `csaStep_eq`.  Each padded input bit `A.getD i (const false)` has depth at
++most `max 1 (maxDepth (A ++ B ++ C))` (`depth_le_maxDepth` for a member, `depth_const` for the
++padding).  Then `depth_xor3_le` bounds every `S_i` by that plus `4`, `depth_maj3_le` every
++`K_{i+1}` by that plus `3`, and `depth_const` gives `K_0` depth `1`; a list induction with
++`maxDepth_cons`/`maxDepth_nil` (and `maxDepth` of an append) bounds the maximum. -/
++theorem depth_csaStep_le (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    TreeCircuit.maxDepth ((csaStep A B C).1 ++ (csaStep A B C).2) ≤
++      max 1 (TreeCircuit.maxDepth (A ++ B ++ C)) + 4 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F5, fan-in two.** A carry-save step adds only fan-in-2 gates:
++`TreeCircuit.maxFaninL ((csaStep A B C).1 ++ (csaStep A B C).2) ≤
++max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL (A ++ B ++ C))`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `csaStep_eq`; each padded input bit has fan-in at most
++`maxFaninL (A ++ B ++ C)` (`maxFanin_le_maxFaninL`, `maxFanin_const`), and
++`maxFanin_xor3_le`, `maxFanin_maj3_le` bound every output bit; a list induction with
++`maxFaninL_cons`/`maxFaninL_nil` bounds the maximum. -/
++theorem maxFaninL_csaStep_le (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    TreeCircuit.maxFaninL ((csaStep A B C).1 ++ (csaStep A B C).2) ≤
++      max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL (A ++ B ++ C)) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-! ### Reduction to two numbers (F6) -/
++
++/-- One carry-save layer over a list of numbers: every consecutive triple `A, B, C` is replaced
++by the two words of `csaStep A B C`, and a remainder of one or two numbers is kept,
++`csaLayer (A :: B :: C :: Us) = (csaStep A B C).1 :: (csaStep A B C).2 :: csaLayer Us` and
++`csaLayer Us = Us` for `|Us| ≤ 2`.  It sends `k` numbers to `2 ⌊k / 3⌋ + k % 3`.  Glue. -/
++def csaLayer : List (List (TreeCircuit n)) → List (List (TreeCircuit n))
++  | A :: B :: C :: Us => (csaStep A B C).1 :: (csaStep A B C).2 :: csaLayer Us
++  | Us => Us
++
++/-- A carry-save layer replaces the first triple by its two words:
++`csaLayer (A :: B :: C :: Us) = (csaStep A B C).1 :: (csaStep A B C).2 :: csaLayer Us`. -/
++theorem csaLayer_cons_cons_cons (A B C : List (TreeCircuit n))
++    (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) :
++    csaLayer (A :: B :: C :: Us) = (csaStep A B C).1 :: (csaStep A B C).2 :: csaLayer Us := rfl
++
++/-- A carry-save layer keeps a list of at most two numbers:
++`Us.length ≤ 2 → csaLayer Us = Us`. -/
++theorem csaLayer_of_length_le_two {Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))} (h : Us.length ≤ 2) :
++    csaLayer Us = Us := by
++  match Us, h with
++  | [], _ => rfl
++  | [_], _ => rfl
++  | [_, _], _ => rfl
++  | _ :: _ :: _ :: _, h => simp only [List.length_cons] at h; omega
++
++/-- The number of carry-save layers with fuel `f`: zero once at most two numbers are left,
++`csaLevelsAux (f + 1) k = if k ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1`. -/
++private def csaLevelsAux : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ
++  | 0, _ => 0
++  | f + 1, k => if k ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1
++
++/-- The fuel does not matter once it is at least the count. -/
++private theorem csaLevelsAux_fuel :
++    ∀ f g k : ℕ, k ≤ f → k ≤ g → csaLevelsAux f k = csaLevelsAux g k
++  | 0, 0, _, _, _ => rfl
++  | 0, g + 1, k, hf, _ => by
++      obtain rfl : k = 0 := by omega
++      show 0 = if 0 ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux g (2 * (0 / 3) + 0 % 3) + 1
++      rw [if_pos (by omega)]
++  | f + 1, 0, k, _, hg => by
++      obtain rfl : k = 0 := by omega
++      show (if 0 ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * (0 / 3) + 0 % 3) + 1) = 0
++      rw [if_pos (by omega)]
++  | f + 1, g + 1, k, hf, hg => by
++      show (if k ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1) =
++        (if k ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux g (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1)
++      by_cases hk : k ≤ 2
++      · rw [if_pos hk, if_pos hk]
++      · rw [if_neg hk, if_neg hk,
++          csaLevelsAux_fuel f g (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) (by omega) (by omega)]
++
++/-- The number of carry-save layers that reduce `k` numbers to at most two: `0` for `k ≤ 2`,
++and `csaLevels (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1` for `k > 2` (`csaLevels_of_le_two`,
++`csaLevels_of_two_lt`).  Computed with fuel `k`, by structural recursion.  Glue. -/
++def csaLevels (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
++  csaLevelsAux k k
++
++/-- No carry-save layer is needed for at most two numbers: `k ≤ 2 → csaLevels k = 0`. -/
++theorem csaLevels_of_le_two {k : ℕ} (h : k ≤ 2) : csaLevels k = 0 := by
++  match k, h with
++  | 0, _ => rfl
++  | f + 1, h =>
++      show (if f + 1 ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * ((f + 1) / 3) + (f + 1) % 3) + 1) = 0
++      rw [if_pos h]
++
++/-- More than two numbers need one layer more than their image under a layer:
++`2 < k → csaLevels k = csaLevels (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1`. -/
++theorem csaLevels_of_two_lt {k : ℕ} (h : 2 < k) :
++    csaLevels k = csaLevels (2 * (k / 3) + k % 3) + 1 := by
++  match k, h with
++  | f + 1, h =>
++      show (if f + 1 ≤ 2 then 0 else csaLevelsAux f (2 * ((f + 1) / 3) + (f + 1) % 3) + 1) =
++        csaLevelsAux (2 * ((f + 1) / 3) + (f + 1) % 3) (2 * ((f + 1) / 3) + (f + 1) % 3) + 1
++      rw [if_neg (by omega), csaLevelsAux_fuel f _ _ (by omega) le_rfl]
++
++/-- The carry-save reduction of a list of numbers to two: apply `csaLayer`
++`csaLevels |Us|` times and read the (at most two) numbers left, padding with `[]`,
++`csaReduce Us = (Vs.getD 0 [], Vs.getD 1 [])` with `Vs = Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels |Us|) Us`.
++`val (csaReduce Us).1 + val (csaReduce Us).2 = Σ val Us` (`csaReduce_val`).
++[BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof, p029-b002]; the carry-save tree is r3 §H3.4's deviation. -/
++def csaReduce (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) : List (TreeCircuit n) × List (TreeCircuit n) :=
++  ((Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels Us.length) Us).getD 0 [],
++    (Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels Us.length) Us).getD 1 [])
++
++/-- The reduction reads the first two numbers after `csaLevels |Us|` layers:
++`csaReduce Us = (Vs.getD 0 [], Vs.getD 1 [])` with
++`Vs = Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels Us.length) Us`. -/
++theorem csaReduce_eq (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) :
++    csaReduce Us =
++      ((Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels Us.length) Us).getD 0 [],
++        (Nat.repeat csaLayer (csaLevels Us.length) Us).getD 1 []) := rfl
++
++/-- **F6, value.** The reduction keeps the total: for every assignment `x`,
++`val x (csaReduce Us).1 + val x (csaReduce Us).2 = (Us.map (val x)).sum`.
++[BV11, Lemma 4.5 proof, p029-b002]; *deviation:* the carry-save tree (r3 §H3.4).
++
++**Proof sketch.** (1) One layer keeps the total, `((csaLayer Us).map (val x)).sum =
++(Us.map (val x)).sum`: induction along `csaLayer`'s cases (`csaLayer_cons_cons_cons`,
++`csaLayer_of_length_le_two`) with `csaStep_val` for each triple.  (2) One layer sends length
++`k` to `2 (k / 3) + k % 3` (same induction, `List.length_cons`).  (3) Induction on `k` along
++`csaLevels_of_le_two`/`csaLevels_of_two_lt`: after `csaLevels k` layers at most two numbers are
++left, with the original total.  (4) For a list of length at most two the total is
++`getD 0 [] + getD 1 []`, as `val x [] = 0` (`val_eq`, `bitsVal` of `[]` is `0` by
++`bitsVal_eq_prefixVal` and `prefixVal_zero`). -/
++theorem csaReduce_val (x : Fin n → Bool) (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) :
++    val x (csaReduce Us).1 + val x (csaReduce Us).2 = (Us.map (val x)).sum := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F6, depth.** The two numbers lie at most `4 · csaLevels |Us|` levels above the inputs
++(counted from at least `1`): `TreeCircuit.maxDepth ((csaReduce Us).1 ++ (csaReduce Us).2) ≤
++max 1 (TreeCircuit.maxDepth Us.flatten) + 4 * csaLevels Us.length`.  r3's
++"`depth ≤ 4·csaLevels k + d₀`" *(fix)*, settled with `d₀ = max 1 (input depth)`.  No term
++depends on the bit length.
++
++**Proof sketch.** With `D = max 1 (maxDepth Us.flatten)`: one layer raises the bound by at
++most `4` (`csaLayer`'s cases, `depth_csaStep_le` for each triple, the kept remainder
++unchanged), so `j` layers give `D + 4 j` (induction on `j`, `Nat.repeat`); with
++`j = csaLevels |Us|` the two read-off numbers are members (or `[]`) of the final list
++(`List.getD`, `maxDepth_nil`). -/
++theorem depth_csaReduce_le (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) :
++    TreeCircuit.maxDepth ((csaReduce Us).1 ++ (csaReduce Us).2) ≤
++      max 1 (TreeCircuit.maxDepth Us.flatten) + 4 * csaLevels Us.length := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F6, fan-in two.** The reduction adds only fan-in-2 gates:
++`TreeCircuit.maxFaninL ((csaReduce Us).1 ++ (csaReduce Us).2) ≤
++max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL Us.flatten)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** As `depth_csaReduce_le`, with `maxFaninL_csaStep_le` for each triple: the
++bound `max 2 (maxFaninL Us.flatten)` is kept by every layer. -/
++theorem maxFaninL_csaReduce_le (Us : List (List (TreeCircuit n))) :
++    TreeCircuit.maxFaninL ((csaReduce Us).1 ++ (csaReduce Us).2) ≤
++      max 2 (TreeCircuit.maxFaninL Us.flatten) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F6, the number of layers.** At most `2⌈log₂ k⌉` carry-save layers reduce `k` numbers to
++two: `csaLevels k ≤ 2 * Nat.clog 2 k`.  Glue (r3 §H5.7, F6); r3's draft constant
++`2⌈log₂ k⌉ + 3` *(fix)* is settled at `2⌈log₂ k⌉`, which holds (probe P10: kernel `decide`
++for `k < 512` against a structural surrogate of `Nat.clog 2`, `#eval` for `k < 100000`).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Strong induction on `k`.  For `k ≤ 2`, `csaLevels_of_le_two`.  For `k > 2`,
++let `k₁ = 2 (k / 3) + k % 3`; if `k₁ ≤ 2` then `csaLevels k = 1 ≤ 2 ≤ 2⌈log₂ k⌉`.  Otherwise
++`csaLevels k = csaLevels k₂ + 2` with `k₂ = 2 (k₁ / 3) + k₁ % 3` (`csaLevels_of_two_lt` twice),
++and `k₂ ≤ ⌈k / 2⌉` (by `omega` for `k ≥ 20`, from `k₂ ≤ (4k + 10) / 9`, and by cases below), so
++`⌈log₂ k₂⌉ ≤ ⌈log₂ k⌉ - 1` (`Nat.clog_mono_right`, `Nat.clog_of_two_le`) and the induction
++hypothesis closes it. -/
++theorem csaLevels_le (k : ℕ) : csaLevels k ≤ 2 * Nat.clog 2 k := by
++  sorry
++
++end FormulaArith
++
++end BoolCircuit

++/-! ### De Morgan negation and substitution (F1–F2) -/
++
++mutual
++
++/-- The De Morgan negation of a formula: every literal flips its sign and every gate flips its
++kind, `(lit ⟨i, s⟩).neg = lit ⟨i, !s⟩` and `(node b cs).neg = node (!b) (cs.map neg)`, so
++that `c.neg` computes `!c.eval x` with the same shape, depth and fan-in
++(`TreeCircuit.neg_eval`, `depth_neg`, `maxFanin_neg`).  Structural recursion over the nested
++child list, through the private list helper `negList`.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.neg : TreeCircuit n → TreeCircuit n
++  | .lit l => .lit ⟨l.idx, !l.sign⟩
++  | .node b cs => .node (!b) (negList cs)
++
++/-- `TreeCircuit.neg` on every member of a child list: `negList cs = cs.map neg`. -/
++private def negList : List (TreeCircuit n) → List (TreeCircuit n)
++  | [] => []
++  | c :: cs => c.neg :: negList cs
++
++end
++
++/-- The negation of a literal flips its sign:
++`(TreeCircuit.lit l).neg = TreeCircuit.lit ⟨l.idx, !l.sign⟩`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.neg_lit (l : Lit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.lit l).neg = TreeCircuit.lit ⟨l.idx, !l.sign⟩ := rfl
++
++/-- The list helper of `neg` is `List.map`: `negList cs = cs.map TreeCircuit.neg`. -/
++private theorem negList_eq_map : ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n), negList cs = cs.map TreeCircuit.neg
++  | [] => rfl
++  | c :: cs => congrArg (c.neg :: ·) (negList_eq_map cs)
++
++/-- The negation of a gate flips its kind and negates every child:
++`(TreeCircuit.node b cs).neg = TreeCircuit.node (!b) (cs.map TreeCircuit.neg)`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.neg_node (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n)) :
++    (TreeCircuit.node b cs).neg = TreeCircuit.node (!b) (cs.map TreeCircuit.neg) :=
++  congrArg (TreeCircuit.node (!b)) (negList_eq_map cs)
++
++/-- **F1, value.** The De Morgan negation computes the negation:
++`c.neg.eval x = !c.eval x`.  Glue (r3 §H5.7, F1).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`).  A literal: `neg_lit`, then
++`eval_lit` and the value of a literal (`Lit.eval`, whose reader is missing; see the census)
++with the sign flipped.  A gate `node b cs`: `neg_node`; for `b = true` the negated gate is an
++`∨`-gate, so `eval_node_false` gives `(cs.map neg).any (·.eval x)`, which by the induction
++hypothesis is `cs.any (! ·.eval x) = !(cs.all (·.eval x))`, that is `!` of `eval_node_true`;
++`b = false` is symmetric. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.neg_eval (c : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    c.neg.eval x = !c.eval x := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F1, depth.** De Morgan negation preserves the depth: `c.neg.depth = c.depth`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`).  A literal stays a literal (depth `0`
++on both sides; the reader `depth_lit` is missing, see the census).  A gate: `neg_node` and
++`depth_node` reduce the claim to `maxDepth (cs.map neg) = maxDepth cs`, an induction on the
++list with `maxDepth_nil`/`maxDepth_cons` and the induction hypothesis on each child. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_neg (c : TreeCircuit n) : c.neg.depth = c.depth := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F1, fan-in.** De Morgan negation preserves the fan-in: `c.neg.maxFanin = c.maxFanin`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** As for `depth_neg`: induction on `c`; a gate keeps its arity
++(`List.length_map`), and `maxFanin_node` with `maxFaninL_nil`/`maxFaninL_cons` and the
++induction hypothesis gives `maxFaninL (cs.map neg) = maxFaninL cs`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_neg (c : TreeCircuit n) : c.neg.maxFanin = c.maxFanin := by
++  sorry
++
++mutual
++
++/-- Substitution of formulas for variables: `c.subst f` replaces every positive literal
++`x_i` of `c` by `f i` and every negative literal `¬x_i` by `(f i).neg`, keeping the gates,
++`(lit l).subst f = if l.sign then f l.idx else (f l.idx).neg` and
++`(node b cs).subst f = node b (cs.map (·.subst f))`.  It computes `c` on the values of the
++`f i` (`TreeCircuit.subst_eval`).  Structural recursion over the nested child list, through
++the private list helper `substList`.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.subst {m : ℕ} : TreeCircuit n → (Fin n → TreeCircuit m) → TreeCircuit m
++  | .lit l, f => if l.sign then f l.idx else (f l.idx).neg
++  | .node b cs, f => .node b (substList cs f)
++
++/-- `TreeCircuit.subst` on every member of a child list:
++`substList cs f = cs.map (·.subst f)`. -/
++private def substList {m : ℕ} :
++    List (TreeCircuit n) → (Fin n → TreeCircuit m) → List (TreeCircuit m)
++  | [], _ => []
++  | c :: cs, f => c.subst f :: substList cs f
++
++end
++
++/-- Substitution into a literal: the positive literal `x_i` becomes `f i` and the negative
++literal `¬x_i` becomes `(f i).neg`,
++`(TreeCircuit.lit l).subst f = if l.sign then f l.idx else (f l.idx).neg`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.subst_lit {m : ℕ} (l : Lit n) (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m) :
++    (TreeCircuit.lit l).subst f = if l.sign then f l.idx else (f l.idx).neg := rfl
++
++/-- The list helper of `subst` is `List.map`: `substList cs f = cs.map (·.subst f)`. -/
++private theorem substList_eq_map {m : ℕ} (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m) :
++    ∀ cs : List (TreeCircuit n), substList cs f = cs.map fun c => c.subst f
++  | [] => rfl
++  | c :: cs => congrArg (c.subst f :: ·) (substList_eq_map f cs)
++
++/-- Substitution into a gate substitutes into every child:
++`(TreeCircuit.node b cs).subst f = TreeCircuit.node b (cs.map fun c => c.subst f)`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.subst_node {m : ℕ} (b : Bool) (cs : List (TreeCircuit n))
++    (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m) :
++    (TreeCircuit.node b cs).subst f = TreeCircuit.node b (cs.map fun c => c.subst f) :=
++  congrArg (TreeCircuit.node b) (substList_eq_map f cs)
++
++/-- **F2, value.** Substitution computes the formula on the substituted values:
++`(c.subst f).eval x = c.eval fun i => (f i).eval x`.  Glue (r3 §H5.7, F2).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` (`TreeCircuit.ind`).  A positive literal: `subst_lit`, and
++both sides are `(f i).eval x` (`eval_lit` and the literal's value).  A negative literal: the
++left side is `(f i).neg.eval x = !(f i).eval x` by `neg_eval`, which is the value of `¬x_i`
++under `fun i => (f i).eval x`.  A gate: `subst_node`, then `eval_node_true`/`eval_node_false`
++on both sides, `List.all_map`/`List.any_map` and the induction hypothesis on each child. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.subst_eval {m : ℕ} (c : TreeCircuit n) (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m)
++    (x : Fin m → Bool) : (c.subst f).eval x = c.eval fun i => (f i).eval x := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F2, depth.** Substitution adds at most the depth of the deepest substituted formula:
++`(c.subst f).depth ≤ c.depth + Finset.univ.sup fun i => (f i).depth`.  Glue (r3 §H5.7, F2).
++
++*Deviation from r3's sketch:* r3 writes `⨆ i, (f i).depth`; `SupSet ℕ` is not in this file's
++(frozen) import closure, so the supremum over the finite index type is the equal
++`Finset.univ.sup`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c`, with `D = Finset.univ.sup fun i => (f i).depth`.  A
++literal: the result is `f i` or `(f i).neg`, of depth `(f i).depth ≤ D`
++(`depth_neg`, `Finset.le_sup`), and `c.depth = 0`.  A gate: `subst_node` and `depth_node` give
++`1 + maxDepth (cs.map (·.subst f))`; each child is bounded by `child.depth + D` (induction
++hypothesis) `≤ maxDepth cs + D` (`depth_le_maxDepth`), so the list maximum is too
++(`maxDepth_nil`/`maxDepth_cons`), and `depth_node` on `c` closes it. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_subst_le {m : ℕ} (c : TreeCircuit n) (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m) :
++    (c.subst f).depth ≤ c.depth + Finset.univ.sup fun i => (f i).depth := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F2, fan-in.** Substitution has the fan-in of the formula or of a substituted formula:
++`(c.subst f).maxFanin ≤ max c.maxFanin (Finset.univ.sup fun i => (f i).maxFanin)`.  Glue
++(r3 §H5.7, F2; r3 names the lemma without a formula, and `⨆` is written `Finset.univ.sup` as
++in `depth_subst_le`).
++
++**Proof sketch.** Induction on `c` as in `depth_subst_le`.  A literal becomes `f i` or
++`(f i).neg`, of fan-in `(f i).maxFanin` (`maxFanin_neg`, `Finset.le_sup`).  A gate keeps its
++arity (`subst_node`, `List.length_map`), and `maxFanin_node`, `maxFaninL_cons` and the
++induction hypothesis bound the children. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_subst_le {m : ℕ} (c : TreeCircuit n) (f : Fin n → TreeCircuit m) :
++    (c.subst f).maxFanin ≤ max c.maxFanin (Finset.univ.sup fun i => (f i).maxFanin) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-! ### Constants and the fan-in-2 gadgets (F3) -/
++
++/-- The constant formula with value `b`: the empty gate, `TreeCircuit.const b = node b []`
++(an empty `∧` is `true`, an empty `∨` is `false`).  Depth `1`, fan-in `0`.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.const (b : Bool) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  .node b []
++
++/-- The constant formula is the empty gate:
++`(TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n) = TreeCircuit.node b []`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.const_eq (b : Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n) = TreeCircuit.node b [] := rfl
++
++/-- **F3, constant: value.** The constant formula evaluates to its constant:
++`(TreeCircuit.const b).eval x = b`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `const_eq`, then `eval_node_true` (`[].all _ = true`) for `b = true` and
++`eval_node_false` (`[].any _ = false`) for `b = false`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.const_eval (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).eval x = b := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, constant: depth.** The constant formula has depth one:
++`(TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).depth = 1`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `const_eq`, `depth_node` and `maxDepth_nil`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_const (b : Bool) : (TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).depth = 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, constant: fan-in.** The constant formula has fan-in zero:
++`(TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).maxFanin = 0`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `const_eq`, `maxFanin_node`, `List.length_nil` and `maxFaninL_nil`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_const (b : Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.const b : TreeCircuit n).maxFanin = 0 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- The binary conjunction `TreeCircuit.and2 a b = node true [a, b]`.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.and2 (a b : TreeCircuit n) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  .node true [a, b]
++
++/-- The binary conjunction is a two-child `∧`-gate:
++`TreeCircuit.and2 a b = TreeCircuit.node true [a, b]`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.and2_eq (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.and2 a b = TreeCircuit.node true [a, b] := rfl
++
++/-- The binary disjunction `TreeCircuit.or2 a b = node false [a, b]`.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.or2 (a b : TreeCircuit n) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  .node false [a, b]
++
++/-- The binary disjunction is a two-child `∨`-gate:
++`TreeCircuit.or2 a b = TreeCircuit.node false [a, b]`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.or2_eq (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.or2 a b = TreeCircuit.node false [a, b] := rfl
++
++/-- The binary exclusive or `TreeCircuit.xor2 a b = or2 (and2 a b.neg) (and2 a.neg b)`, that
++is `(a ∧ ¬b) ∨ (¬a ∧ b)` with the negations by De Morgan (`neg`).  Two levels above its
++arguments.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.xor2 (a b : TreeCircuit n) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b.neg) (TreeCircuit.and2 a.neg b)
++
++/-- The exclusive or as a formula:
++`TreeCircuit.xor2 a b = TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b.neg) (TreeCircuit.and2 a.neg b)`.
++-/
++theorem TreeCircuit.xor2_eq (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.xor2 a b = TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b.neg) (TreeCircuit.and2 a.neg b) :=
++  rfl
++
++/-- The three-input exclusive or (the full adder's sum bit)
++`TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c = xor2 (xor2 a b) c`.  Four levels above its arguments.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.xor3 (a b c : TreeCircuit n) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  TreeCircuit.xor2 (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b) c
++
++/-- The three-input exclusive or as nested binary ones:
++`TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c = TreeCircuit.xor2 (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b) c`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.xor3_eq (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c = TreeCircuit.xor2 (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b) c := rfl
++
++/-- The three-input majority (the full adder's carry bit)
++`TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c = or2 (and2 a b) (and2 c (or2 a b))`, that is
++`(a ∧ b) ∨ (c ∧ (a ∨ b))`.  Three levels above its arguments.  Glue. -/
++def TreeCircuit.maj3 (a b c : TreeCircuit n) : TreeCircuit n :=
++  TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b) (TreeCircuit.and2 c (TreeCircuit.or2 a b))
++
++/-- The majority as a formula: `TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c =
++TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b) (TreeCircuit.and2 c (TreeCircuit.or2 a b))`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maj3_eq (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c =
++      TreeCircuit.or2 (TreeCircuit.and2 a b) (TreeCircuit.and2 c (TreeCircuit.or2 a b)) := rfl
++
++/-- **F3, `and2`: value.** `(TreeCircuit.and2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x && b.eval x)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `and2_eq`, `eval_node_true`, and `List.all_cons`/`List.all_nil`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.and2_eval (a b : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.and2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x && b.eval x) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `and2`: depth `+1`.** `(TreeCircuit.and2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 1`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `and2_eq`, `depth_node`, `maxDepth_cons` twice and `maxDepth_nil`; then
++`omega` (it is an equality). -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_and2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.and2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `and2`: fan-in two.**
++`(TreeCircuit.and2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `and2_eq`, `maxFanin_node` (arity `2`), `maxFaninL_cons` twice and
++`maxFaninL_nil`; then `omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_and2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.and2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `or2`: value.** `(TreeCircuit.or2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x || b.eval x)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `or2_eq`, `eval_node_false`, and `List.any_cons`/`List.any_nil`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.or2_eval (a b : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.or2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x || b.eval x) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `or2`: depth `+1`.** `(TreeCircuit.or2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 1`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** As `depth_and2_le`, through `or2_eq`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_or2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.or2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 1 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `or2`: fan-in two.**
++`(TreeCircuit.or2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** As `maxFanin_and2_le`, through `or2_eq`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_or2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.or2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor2`: value.** `(TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor2_eq`, `or2_eval`, `and2_eval` and `neg_eval`; then a case split on the
++two Booleans. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.xor2_eval (a b : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).eval x = (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor2`: depth `+2`.** `(TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 2`.
++Glue; the constant `2` is settled by the construction (one `∧` level, one `∨` level, and the
++depth-preserving `neg`).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor2_eq`, `depth_or2_le`, `depth_and2_le` twice, and `depth_neg` for
++`a.neg`, `b.neg`; then `omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_xor2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).depth ≤ max a.depth b.depth + 2 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor2`: fan-in two.**
++`(TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin)`.  Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor2_eq`, `maxFanin_or2_le`, `maxFanin_and2_le` and `maxFanin_neg`; then
++`omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_xor2_le (a b : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor2 a b).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin b.maxFanin) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor3`: value** (the full adder's sum bit).
++`(TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).eval x = (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x ^^ c.eval x)`.  Glue (r3 §H5.7, F3).
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor3_eq` and `xor2_eval` twice. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.xor3_eval (a b c : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).eval x = (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x ^^ c.eval x) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor3`: depth `+4`.**
++`(TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).depth ≤ max a.depth (max b.depth c.depth) + 4`.  Glue; r3's `+4`
++*(fix)*, settled by the construction: two nested `xor2`, each `+2`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor3_eq`, `depth_xor2_le` twice; then `omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_xor3_le (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).depth ≤ max a.depth (max b.depth c.depth) + 4 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `xor3`: fan-in two.**
++`(TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin (max b.maxFanin c.maxFanin))`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `xor3_eq` and `maxFanin_xor2_le` twice; then `omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_xor3_le (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.xor3 a b c).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin (max b.maxFanin c.maxFanin)) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `maj3`: value** (the full adder's carry bit, in the form of `carryBit`'s step).
++`(TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).eval x = (a.eval x && b.eval x || c.eval x && (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x))`.
++Glue (r3 §H5.7, F3).  The formula computes `(a ∧ b) ∨ (c ∧ (a ∨ b))`, which is the same
++Boolean function.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `maj3_eq`, `or2_eval`, `and2_eval` twice and `or2_eval` again; then a case
++split on the three Booleans. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maj3_eval (a b c : TreeCircuit n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
++    (TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).eval x =
++      (a.eval x && b.eval x || c.eval x && (a.eval x ^^ b.eval x)) := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `maj3`: depth `+3`.**
++`(TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).depth ≤ max a.depth (max b.depth c.depth) + 3`.  Glue; r3's `+3`
++*(fix)*, settled by the construction `(a ∧ b) ∨ (c ∧ (a ∨ b))`.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `maj3_eq`, `depth_or2_le` twice and `depth_and2_le` twice; then `omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.depth_maj3_le (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).depth ≤ max a.depth (max b.depth c.depth) + 3 := by
++  sorry
++
++/-- **F3, `maj3`: fan-in two.**
++`(TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin (max b.maxFanin c.maxFanin))`.
++Glue.
++
++**Proof sketch.** `maj3_eq`, `maxFanin_or2_le` twice and `maxFanin_and2_le` twice; then
++`omega`. -/
++theorem TreeCircuit.maxFanin_maj3_le (a b c : TreeCircuit n) :
++    (TreeCircuit.maj3 a b c).maxFanin ≤ max 2 (max a.maxFanin (max b.maxFanin c.maxFanin)) := by
++  sorry
++
